## 2026-07-29 — Lyon's Giant Chestnut of Parc de la Garde: a real 1.8km pin error fixed via the Metropole de Lyon's own remarkable-tree register, now genuinely reachable

New session. `python3 scripts/visitors.py`: 69 visits, 123 page views over 7 days (22-28 July: 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17, 12), consistent with prior sessions today. No reader submissions (CSV header row only). Build clean at start (557 pages). Depth-exit-test bar unchanged at 5/8. Given how exhaustively documented today's many prior sessions already left the standard photo/pin techniques on Amsterdam, Lisbon and Istanbul (dozens of entries above), tested something genuinely new instead: whether this session's own network access differs from prior sessions', since several leads were explicitly left as "worth a retry from a different environment" (RBGE's Living Collection database, Grand Lyon's open data portal, Istanbul's anitagaclar.gov.tr/konusanagac.com).

**Confirmed this session's `curl`/`urllib` (not WebFetch) can reach `data.rbge.org.uk` and `data.grandlyon.com` directly**, unlike prior sessions' tooling. `anitagaclar.gov.tr` still times out and `konusanagac.com` still fails DNS resolution, so those two stay genuinely blocked, not just untried.

**RBGE Living Collection database, queried properly for the first time (`?eti=<name>&cfg=livcol.cfg`)**: works and returns real accession-level results (e.g. 15+ wild-collected Cedrus libani, 5 Castanea sativa accessions across RBGE's four gardens). But checked an individual accession page directly (`?cfg=accessions.cfg&acc_num=19923618`) and confirmed it carries no bed, garden-location or coordinate data in the public interface, only species, provenance and collection origin. This is a different, more precise finding than "blocked": the database is reachable and queryable, it simply does not hold the location data this project needs to tighten Edinburgh's `edi_002`/`edi_004` pins. Not worth re-querying for pin work; a future session shouldn't re-flag this as merely untried.

**Grand Lyon's WFS server (`download.data.grandlyon.com/wfs/grandlyon`), a real find**: fetched the full GetCapabilities (627 feature types) and located the exact layer a 2026-07-29 entry had named but never reached: `metropole-de-lyon:plu_h_opposable.pluboispt`, titled "Espace boise classe ponctuel - Arbre remarquable du PLU-H de la Metropole de Lyon", a 429-point dataset of individually registered remarkable trees (species, street-level location text, health note, exact coordinates). Queried all 429 features and matched against Lyon's 5 remaining approximate pins by species + street name:

- **lyo_004 (Giant Chestnut of Parc de la Garde): fixed, upgraded to `confirmed`.** A single Aesculus hippocastanum record at "Rue de la Garde", "tres grand exemplaire en bonne sante" ("very large specimen in good health", matching this entry's own "enormous trunk" description), at 45.76073066, 4.78922252. Cross-checked Parc de la Garde's real location independently via Nominatim (45.7602, 4.7908, "Parc de la Garde, Champvert-Point du jour, Lyon 5e"): the registry point sits within 90m of it. The entry's prior stored coordinate (45.744, 4.788) was roughly 1.8km south, outside the park entirely, a real error rather than honest approximation. This entry's own story already claims the national "Arbre Remarquable" label; this register is exactly that label's own data, the strongest kind of match available.
- **lyo_005 (Parc de Gerland), lyo_008 (Parc de la Cerisaie): genuine dead ends.** Zero records anywhere in the 429-point dataset mention "Gerland", "Coubertin" or "Cerisaie" in their location text.
- **lyo_006 (Atlas Cedar, Parc de la Chapelle): correctly not applied.** 4 records matched "Chapelle" but all are Quercus pedunculatus (pedunculate oak, wrong species) at "Hameau de la Chapelle", a rural hamlet roughly 15km north of the city (coordinates ~45.906), a different "Chapelle" place entirely, not 69009 Lyon.
- **lyo_009 (Garden Trees of Jardin des Chartreux): a real corroboration, pin left unchanged on purpose.** One record, a Juglans (hickory) at "Jardin des Chartreux, Cours du General Giraud", confirms the garden itself is a genuine registered remarkable-tree site, independent support for the entry's own framing. Not used to move the pin: this entry is deliberately written as an ensemble with "no individual tree named or dated" (its own `notes` field), so pinning it to one specific hickory's coordinate would misrepresent the entry as pointing at a single identified tree it explicitly isn't.

Lyon now has 4 approximate pins (lyo_005, lyo_006, lyo_008, lyo_009), down from 5, a genuine confirmed pin fix rather than another exhausted search. Site rebuilt, all contracts validated (557 pages), committed and pushed.

**Same session, continued: Athens and Istanbul's own previously-blocked leads retried, both now closed with a sharper reason than "blocked".**

- **Athens' `athenstrees.cityofathens.gr` (a 2026-07-29 entry above confirmed this app's JS-rendered content is unreachable through WebFetch specifically).** This session fetched the raw JS bundle directly and found the real API surface: base path `/api`, with concrete endpoints `/tree-plantings` and `/tree-types` visible in the minified source. Querying `https://athenstrees.cityofathens.gr/api/tree-plantings` and `/api/tree-types` directly both return a clean `HTTP 401 Unauthorized`, not a network or JS-parsing failure. **This is a sharper, more final finding than "blocked": the API exists, is reachable, and requires credentials this project does not have and should not try to obtain.** Not worth another session's time on this exact site; genuinely closed.
- **Istanbul's İBB open data portal (`data.ibb.gov.tr`), reachable this session via its CKAN API** (`api/3/action/package_search`), unlike a prior session's `ENOTFOUND`/`ECONNREFUSED` on the two specific tree-registry URLs it tried. Searched the full catalogue for "ağaç" (tree): 11 real datasets exist, all either city-wide canopy-loss statistics or aggregate district-level counts of trees planted/maintained (e.g. "Park Bahçe ve Yeşil Alan Verileri"), none an individually-named monumental-tree register. Confirmed there is no such dataset on this portal, a genuine content gap rather than a tooling wall. `anitagaclar.gov.tr` (times out) and `konusanagac.com` (DNS failure) both stay blocked even from this session's network; still worth a retry from a different environment specifically for those two, but not via the İBB open data portal, which has been checked properly now and has nothing.
- **RBGE's Living Collection database (`data.rbge.org.uk/living/`), the third lead flagged as "worth trying from elsewhere"**: reachable and its search form works when queried directly by GET (`?eti=<name>&cfg=livcol.cfg`, e.g. returns 15+ Cedrus libani and 5 Castanea sativa accessions). But an individual accession page (`?cfg=accessions.cfg&acc_num=...`) carries no bed, garden-location or coordinate field in the public interface, only species and collection provenance. This closes the lead for good: it is not blocked, it simply does not hold the data (which garden, which bed) this project would need to tighten Edinburgh's `edi_002`/`edi_004` pins.

Three leads that sat as "environment-blocked, retry elsewhere" across recent sessions are now each resolved to a specific, final reason (an API needing auth we don't have; a portal with no matching dataset; a database with no location field), so no future session re-spends time on any of them expecting a different outcome.

**Same session: approved-photo spot-check extended by 2 (23 of 206 now checked), both genuine matches, before Wikimedia's rate limit closed the technique off for this session.** Deliberately sampled from cities untouched by the two prior spot-check rounds (which covered Amsterdam, Athens, Barcelona, Berlin, Brussels, Dublin, Edinburgh, Florence, Kyoto, Lisbon, London, Madrid, Munich, Nice, Paris, Prague, Rome, Seville, Tokyo, Venice, Vienna): Bologna's Sequoia of the Giardini Margherita (`bol_002`, a multi-trunked coast redwood, stringy reddish bark visible, consistent with the species) and Cadiz's Ficus of Alameda Apodaca (`cad_001`, unmistakable Moreton Bay Fig buttress roots and canopy against the seafront balustrade the story describes). After these 2, every further raw `upload.wikimedia.org` fetch this session returned `429 Too many requests`, even after backoff retries, the same wall prior sessions hit; not a new finding, just confirming it's still active. A planned batch of 11 more (Granada, Istanbul, Malaga, Milan, Naples, New York, Palermo, Porto, Sintra, Valencia, Verona) never got past the rate limit; worth resuming from a session with a cooled-off limit rather than retried immediately.

## 2026-07-29 — Two open Naples news threads from 2026-07-28's sweep resolved: both check out clean

Same session, after the photo spot-check. Rung 3 (something published might be wrong) outranks everything else on the ladder, and a 2026-07-28 entry had left two Naples threads explicitly open rather than closed (see "Open threads" list, same date). Chased both down directly rather than leaving them to accumulate.

- **nap_008 (Pine of Posillipo)**: the entry already honestly describes today's tree as a 1995 Legambiente replanting of the famous pine that died in 1984 (the earlier open thread worried this specimen might be caught up in a wider district pine dieback). Confirmed via direct search: the 70-pine felling reported in la Repubblica/Il Mattino in early 2026 is specifically Via Manzoni and Parco Virgiliano, a different part of the Posillipo district from Via Minucio Felice near Chiesa di Sant'Antonio, where this entry's own tree stands. Also incidentally corrected my own understanding, not the data: multiple fresh sources confirm 1995 as the replanting year (Legambiente celebrates the anniversary annually), matching the entry's own claim exactly. No change needed; a genuine regional risk exists but not to this specific tree.
- **nap_009 (Plane Tree of the Villa Comunale)**: the open thread was a 2024 5-month closure for tree analysis, with an unclear outcome. Followed up directly: the analysis had a **positive result**, a plane tree initially thought to need felling was saved (informareonline.com, cross-checked against the original ilmattino.it closure report, whose 2019-dated companion article, premiogreencare.org, independently confirms this entry's own tree by its exact dimensions, 428cm girth/18m height, as one of the three catalogued planes there). Confirmed the garden's current access status directly: open daily 7:00-24:00, free entry, as of July 2026, some non-tree structures (Tempietto di Virgilio, Cassa Armonica) still under renovation until November 2026 but not affecting general park access. This entry's own `access: "Free, public park"` is accurate as written; no change needed.

**A third open thread from the same sweep, Rome's rom_006 (Leccio of the Lake, Villa Borghese), was also chased but stays genuinely open**, not closed: the beetle infestation (Cerambyx cerdo larvae hollowing holm oak trunks across "the entire Villa Borghese", some already felled) is real and current (confirmed via multiple 2026 articles), but which specific trees are affected is not named anywhere found, and this session's tooling couldn't get past a 403 block on the most detailed source (romatoday.it) or load a second (a JS-rendered page that returned empty to WebFetch). One mildly reassuring data point: Italy's RAMI registry entry specific to this exact tree (ilregistrodeglialberi.it/9253) was updated as recently as 1 March 2026 and records no health concern (field literally "not available" rather than flagging disease), which is at least evidence nobody who touched this specific tree's record recently found something to report. Not treated as a clean bill of health, just recorded honestly as still open, same status as 2026-07-28, worth a retry with different tooling or a more targeted search for "Giardino del Lago" specifically rather than "Villa Borghese" generally.

Two of three threads now closed rather than left open indefinitely; the third stays honestly open with a slightly sharper picture than before. No data changed. Nothing needs Hidde.

## 2026-07-29 — Dublin's Storm Éowyn thread checked: a real near-miss caught and rejected, no specific risk confirmed for any of the 10 published trees

Same session. The 2026-07-28 news sweep had flagged Dublin as "worth a dedicated re-check" for Storm Éowyn (24 January 2025) damage, incompletely searched that pass. Searched specifically against each of Dublin's 10 trees' actual locations: Phoenix Park (dub_002 Queen Victoria's Sequoia, dub_009 Great Sycamore of Farmleigh), Trinity College Dublin (dub_005 Provost's Plane, dub_006 Champion Planes of New Square), St Anne's Park (dub_007, dub_008), National Botanic Gardens Glasnevin (dub_004), King's Inns (dub_001), Corkagh Park (dub_010), and Newcastle Co. Dublin (dub_003).

**Caught and rejected a real near-miss before it became a false alarm**: a search summary initially returned "'Magnificent' Trinity College Dublin tree collapses" as if it were 2025 storm news. Checked the actual source directly rather than trusting the summary: it is a **2018** event (an Oregon Maple in Library Square, near the Campanile, felled by structural stress, unrelated to any storm), a completely different tree, species, location and year from anything in our data. Both of that era's original 1840s Oregon Maples were already removed by 2018, well before this project's window; TCD's own Front/Library Square carries none of our 10 Dublin entries regardless. Correctly not applied.

**One general Phoenix Park storm-damage report found** (a tree fell through the park's boundary wall onto Blackhorse Avenue, 24 January 2025), but it names no species and Blackhorse Avenue is not near either of our two Phoenix Park entries (Áras an Uachtaráin for the sequoia, Farmleigh for the sycamore), so not treated as evidence against either.

**No damage report found naming St Anne's Park, the National Botanic Gardens Glasnevin, King's Inns, Corkagh Park, or Newcastle specifically**, despite real, dedicated searches for each. This is the same honest position as 2026-07-28: checked and found nothing, not a clean bill of health, since Storm Éowyn's official toll (10,000+ trees down nationally, per the National Trust) makes it entirely plausible individual parks went unreported in searchable press. No data changed.

## 2026-07-29 — Approved-photo spot-check extended: 12 more viewed directly, all genuine matches (21 of 206 now checked)

Same session, after the Palermo photo hunt. A 2026-07-28 entry had done a first-ever spot-check of `approved` photos (9 of 126 then, all clean) and explicitly left it as a partial clearance, worth continuing. Confirmed this session's WebFetch tool can fetch a raw image URL and, even when its own text description fails (very common: the tool routinely tries to read binary JPEG data as text and reports "corrupted"/"unreadable"), it saves the actual image bytes to a local path that the Read tool opens and displays as a real image. This round-trip (WebFetch the raw upload.wikimedia.org URL, then Read the saved local file) is a reliable way to see real pixel content, not just metadata or captions, worth remembering for future sessions.

Sampled 15 approved photos across cities not touched by the 2026-07-28 check (that pass covered Edinburgh, Berlin, Antwerp, Rome, Brussels, Vienna, Tokyo, plus London's Royal Oak and Charlton House Mulberry): Amsterdam, Athens, Barcelona, Dublin, Florence, Kyoto, Lisbon, Madrid, Munich, Nice, Paris, Prague, Seville, Venice, Vienna's Mozart-Platane. **12 of 15 successfully fetched and viewed; all 12 are genuine, confident matches for their tree**, several distinctively so (Dublin's Hungry Tree literally shows a park bench grown into the trunk; Venice's Cypress Ring of San Francesco del Deserto shows exactly the silhouette its name describes; Lisbon's Ajuda Dragon Tree, Vienna's hollow Mozart-Platane and Barcelona's historic Carob-of-the-Viaduct photo, showing the trunk growing directly through Gaudí's stone columns, all unmistakable). Full list of trees confirmed clean: Amsterdam ams_002, Barcelona bcn_002, Dublin dub_001, Florence flo_001, Lisbon lis_002, Madrid mad_001, Munich muc_001, Nice nce_008, Paris par_005, Seville sev_008, Venice ven_004, Vienna vie_005.

**3 of 15 (Athens ath_001, Kyoto kyo_005, Prague prg_001) failed on a genuine tooling limit, not a content problem**: their source files exceed WebFetch's ~10MB size cap (confirmed by the error message itself, `maxContentLength size of 10485760 exceeded`, the same wall a 2026-07-28 Naples entry hit). A smaller Wikimedia thumbnail URL was tried for Athens and returned an unrelated 400 error; worth a retry with a correctly-formed thumbnail URL in a future session rather than assumed unfixable.

Running total: 21 of 206 approved photos spot-checked, zero bad matches found across two independent sessions' worth of checking. No data changed (nothing needed fixing); recording so a future session extends the sample rather than restarting it, and so the size-limited three don't get miscounted as failures of the technique itself.

## 2026-07-29 — Palermo's 2 remaining photo gaps: real candidates found, both correctly rejected on specificity grounds after direct visual verification

Same session, after the source-link audit and the Most Valuable Trees check. Palermo (published 2026-07-28, one day old) sits at 6/8 photos (75%), just under the 80% floor, and had never had a dedicated photo hunt beyond initial research. Both gaps are well-known named landmarks, good odds in principle.

Dispatched a research pass for pal_005 (Dragon Tree of the Palazzina Cinese) and pal_007 (Date Palm of Villa Bonanno), then verified the two strongest candidates myself by actually viewing the image pixels (this session's tooling can fetch an image file directly and view it with the Read tool, not just read page metadata; confirmed this works and used it to check real content rather than trust captions alone):

- **pal_007's candidate** (`Palermo-Villa-Bonanno-bjs-1.jpg`, CC0, captioned "Palm garden of Villa Bonanno") is genuinely a clean, correctly located photo of mature date palms. **Rejected anyway, on the same specificity principle already applied elsewhere on the site** (e.g. Lyon's Parc de la Chapelle cedar, 2026-07-29): this entry's own data is pinned to one AMI-register-confirmed specimen (2.5m circumference, its own coordinate, deliberately written up as "one specific registered specimen rather than the c.400-palm planting as a whole" per its own notes), not the grove as an ensemble. A wide avenue shot of dozens of palms cannot confirm which one is this specific registered tree, so applying it would mean quietly downgrading a precisely-scoped entry to an ensemble claim it was deliberately written not to be. Left `missing`.
- **pal_005's candidate** (a Palazzina Cinese exterior shot, CC BY-SA 4.0, Davide Mauro) was viewed directly: shows the building's facade with palms and a broad-leaved tree visible at the edge of frame, but no tree matching a mature Dragon Tree's distinctive thick trunk and umbrella-shaped leaf crown is identifiable in it. Correctly left `missing` rather than guessed.
- Both entries' own AMI/RAMI register sourcing was reconfirmed solid regardless (age, condition, coordinates), so this is a photo-only gap, not a verification concern.

Palermo stays at 6/8 (75%), a real, now twice-confirmed gap rather than an unsearched one. No data changed; no rebuild needed.

## 2026-07-29 — A site-wide `verified_sources` link audit (never done before, distinct from the photo-URL audit): 11 confirmed dead citations found, one replaced, rest documented

New session. Visitors: 69 visits, 123 page views over 7 days (22-28 July: 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17, 12), consistent with prior weeks. No reader submissions (CSV header row only). Build clean at start, depth-exit-test bar unchanged at 5/8 (Amsterdam, Lisbon, Istanbul still short).

Checked all 34 "below floor" cities' CURATION.md history before picking work: essentially every one (Naples, Valencia, Granada, Istanbul, Lyon, Verona, Brussels, Dublin, New York, Porto, Sintra, Venice, Antwerp, Bologna, Madrid, Malaga, Lisbon, Milan, Seville, Amsterdam, Athens) already carries multiple dead-end passes from earlier today and prior sessions. Rather than re-run exhausted photo/pin hunts, tried two genuinely fresh angles.

**Angle 1: Edinburgh, the worst-neglected UK focus-region city (4/10 photos, 10/10 approximate pins), had never had a dedicated photo/pin research pass despite the 2026-07-28 geocode audit fixing two of its coordinates.** Dispatched a fresh, thorough research pass (Wikimedia text and category search, iNaturalist API by radius with per-photo license checks, Flickr CC, Openverse, Geograph, RBGE/Woodland Trust/Historic Environment Scotland) against its 6 photo-missing trees (edi_003 Cammo Ash, edi_004 Cedar of Lebanon, edi_005 Monkey Puzzles of Lauriston Castle, edi_007 Camperdown Elm of St Cuthbert's, edi_008 Rockface Sycamore of Cramond, edi_010 Corstorphine Sycamore) and all 10 approximate pins. **Genuine dead end, nothing applied:**
- edi_003: one CC-BY iNaturalist photo exists in the right estate, but Cammo is an 85-acre reserve with multiple ash trees and nothing ties the photo to *the* named oldest/largest one specifically; held back rather than published as a guess, consistent with the project's standing bar against same-species-different-individual matches (the Corstorphine/RBGE and Rome/Villa Torlonia precedents).
- edi_010: the exact right tree was found, twice (Flickr, jsutcliffe), correctly distinguished from the RBGE conservation specimen a prior session had rejected. Both times the license is CC BY-NC-SA, excluded by hard rule 4. Worth recording so a future session doesn't rediscover this near-miss and be tempted to use it.
- edi_004, edi_005, edi_007, edi_008: no usable candidate at all after real effort (the edi_004 RBGE Living Collection candidate on iNaturalist turned out to be a misidentified Cedrus atlantica, not libani, so correctly rejected on species grounds).
- No pin-tightening evidence found for any of the 10; all stay `approximate`.

**Angle 2: a full site-wide audit of `verified_sources` URLs, something the project has never done** (the 2026-07-28 audit covered all 177 photo URLs but not the 779 citation links in `verified_sources`). Checked all 779 with concurrent HEAD/GET requests. Confirmed first, before treating any result as real: `verified_sources` is **not rendered anywhere on the live site** (grepped `build_site.py`), so this is an internal provenance/trust question, not a live broken-link bug on any page.

104 of 779 came back non-200, but the overwhelming majority are 403 (bot-blocking, several from domains this project has already confirmed elsewhere are live but script-hostile, e.g. monumentaltrees.com, baumkunde.de), timeouts, or self-signed-certificate SSL errors, none of which mean the source is actually gone, consistent with this project's long-standing distinction between tooling-blocked and content-dead. **Isolated the genuinely conclusive signals (HTTP 404/410): 11 dead links across 9 trees:**
- Edinburgh edi_006 (BBC feed URL) and edi_010 (RBGE gallery page), Granada gra_001, Istanbul ist_008, Nice nce_009, Paris par_004 (2 dead of 4 sources), Rome rom_005 (2 dead of 3), Tokyo tok_001, Venice ven_010.
- Cross-checked each against how many *other* sources the same tree still has. 5 trees (edi_006, par_004, rom_005, tok_001, ven_010) keep 2+ live sources even after the loss, no action needed. 4 trees dropped to a single live source, weakening this project's own two-independent-source bar retroactively: **Granada's Cypress of San Juan de la Cruz, Istanbul's Florya Mastic Tree, Nice's Aleppo Pine Forest of Mont Boron, Edinburgh's Corstorphine Sycamore.**
- Searched for a replacement second source for each of those 4. **One found and applied**: Granada's dead `turgranada.es` link replaced with `patrimonizate.es` (fetched and confirmed directly: independently corroborates the Cupressus lusitanica species and the San Juan de la Cruz planting tradition). **Three genuine dead ends, real searches with real near-misses correctly rejected rather than forced**: a `yesil.istanbul` page looked promising in a search summary but, fetched and read directly, does not actually mention the Florya mastic tree at all (a reminder of why this project always verifies by fetching the real page rather than trusting a search engine's own summary); Nice's `cultivez-vous.nice.fr` and `departement06.fr` candidate pages failed with an SSL handshake error and a 404 respectively; Edinburgh's Wikipedia and Historic Environment Scotland (trove.scot) candidates for Corstorphine either don't mention the tree or returned 403. These three stay on their single remaining live source; worth a retry if a future session finds new search angles, but not worth re-trying these exact URLs.
- The 3 "URL contains control characters" errors my own check script flagged (Amsterdam ams_008/ams_009, Lisbon lis_007/lis_009) are not real bugs: these `verified_sources` entries intentionally embed a descriptive record ID after the API endpoint (e.g. "`.../MapServer/0 (feature id 283, processo KNJ1/405)`") as a citation aid for future re-verification, not a clickable href, and since the field is never rendered as a link, this is harmless. Noting only so a future automated check doesn't misreport these as broken.

Site rebuilt after the one Granada fix, all contracts validated (557 pages). Nothing else needs Hidde.

## 2026-07-29 — Most Valuable Trees collection: checked and confirmed genuinely blocked, not just unattempted; two other backlog collection ideas re-checked, still blocked

Same session, after the Edinburgh and source-link work above. With content-depth and source-hygiene work done, checked PRODUCT_TODO.md item 4's slate before drafting anything: two collections already shipped earlier today (Trees Planted by Kings, Trees With a Known Planting Date, see below), so the remaining Priority-1 idea worth checking was BACKLOG.md idea #4, Most Valuable Trees, explicitly marked as needing new research (documented tree valuations) rather than just recombination.

Dispatched a dedicated research pass across all 34 non-London published cities, local-language queries included (Dutch, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Portuguese, Turkish), plus general "most valuable/expensive tree" roundups, looking for any tree with a real, sourced monetary valuation beyond our existing 2 London entries (Cheapside Plane, Berkeley Square Plane). **Result: zero new qualifying entries.** Real leads chased down and correctly rejected, each fetched at its primary source rather than trusted from a search summary:

- A €341,541 cedar valuation is real, but the tree is in Brescia, not Rome (a search engine had implied Rome relevance incorrectly).

**Also re-verified, by direct script count, that two other Priority-2 backlog ideas remain genuinely blocked, not just old notes going stale:**
- **Trees older than 1000 years** (BACKLOG idea #7, trigger: 4+ qualifying trees): still exactly 3 site-wide (London's Totteridge Yew, Istanbul's Bahçeköy plane, Kyoto's Heian Cedar), unchanged since the last check. No new candidate has cleared 1000 years at a conservative age estimate since.
- **Churchyard Yews Older Than Their Churches** (BACKLOG idea #6, trigger: 3+ UK/Northern European cities): checked every yew on the site (9 across 8 cities). Only Totteridge (London) genuinely fits the "planted before/beside a church, older than the church itself" premise with a real churchyard setting. Edinburgh's Craigmillar yews stand at a castle courtyard, not a church. Dublin's two yews are a private-garden glebe and a botanic-garden avenue, neither a churchyard. Stays at 1 clean match; not close to the trigger.

No files changed by this entry beyond the BACKLOG.md status note. Nothing needs Hidde.

## 2026-07-29 — A second new collection: Trees With a Known Planting Date

Same session, after the best_time sweep. PRODUCT_TODO.md item 4 allows up to two collections per pass, and BACKLOG.md's idea #1 (its own original framing for this exact idea) was still open with a strong candidate pool.

**The bar, and why it's a genuinely different cut than the Kings collection shipped earlier today**: a tree qualifies here only if its OWN planting year (or a narrow, named range) is actually recorded, not merely a current age estimate that implies a rough planting decade. This rules out every legend ("said to have been planted by shogun X," already correctly excluded from Kings too) and, more subtly, rules out ring-count-inferred ages dressed up as if they were a planting record, a real distinction the drafting pass had to apply tree by tree rather than trust a keyword scan. Explicitly instructed to avoid re-shuffling the same 13 trees already in `trees-planted-by-kings.json`; the candidate pool (32+ genuinely dated plantings across the site) turned out large enough that **zero entries overlap between the two collections**.

**14 entries, oldest planting first**: Florence's Micheli Yew (1720, logged by the botanist who planted it in the garden's own record book), Kew's pagoda tree (1762), Buffon's surviving Paris plane (1785), Prague's Plane of Kampa (1813), Paris's Great Plane of Parc Monceau (1814, the year Napoleon abdicated), Edinburgh's Cedar of Lebanon (1826, "a birth certificate few Scottish trees can match" per RBGE's own words), Valencia's El Titan (1852, planted by accident, mistaken for a magnolia), Granada's Generalife sequoias (1854-56), Munich's Reichs- und Friedenseiche (1 July 1871, a named civic peace-festival planting with 12,000 schoolchildren watching), New York's Camperdown Elm (1872, the Marianne Moore rescue story), Berlin's Tiergarten sequoia (1891), Tokyo's Meiji Jingu Gaien ginkgo avenue (1923 transplanting), Milan's Piazza XXIV Maggio oak (1924, planted by a father in memory of his son and the Alpini who didn't survive the war), and Antwerp's 1994 liberation-anniversary linden, the youngest tree on the whole site to carry an exact planting day.

**Spot-checked two of the more surprising claims against the actual source stories before trusting the summary** (Munich's 12,000-schoolchildren peace festival, New York's Marianne Moore rescue campaign): both accurate, nothing embellished beyond what the tree's own already-published story states.

**Rejected candidates worth naming**: contested dates (Madrid's Ahuehuete, disputed between 1633 and after 1814; Milan's ginkgo pair, hedged between ring-count and record) and inferred-not-recorded ages (most of the site's "roughly X years old" entries) were the two failure modes this bar exists to catch, and both showed up in the candidate pool exactly as expected.

Wrote `data/collections/trees-with-known-planting-dates.json`, `status: published`. Site rebuilt: 557 pages, 12 public collections, all contracts validated. Committed and pushed with the rest of today's work.

**Added `best_time` to 3 trees, each verified against its own species and climate rather than a generic textbook answer:**

- **Munich `muc_001`**: Small-leaved Lime, June-July flowering (confirmed a touch later than large-leaved lime).
- **Munich `muc_006`**: Fern-leaved Beech, October golden-bronze, matching the standard beech entry already in the same file.

**Correctly left alone after real checking, not just skipped on a hunch**: Berlin's and Munich's European White Elms and Munich's Wych Elm (sourced as "not very spectacular"/"dull yellow"), Edinburgh's European Ash ("no marked autumn colour"), Edinburgh's two weeping-form elm cultivars (valued for shape, no sourced colour claim), and, the one genuine climate trap of this pass, **Naples' Montezuma Cypress**: the species is only reliably deciduous and colourful in cold climates and stays evergreen or semi-evergreen in a Mediterranean one, so assigning it an autumn moment here would have been exactly the hemisphere/climate-mismatch mistake the standing rule warns against. Plane trees and Mediterranean Hackberry, present across nearly every city, stayed untouched site-wide, consistent with the pattern already established for Paris and Rome's own entries (muted, brown seasonal change, not a real "go now" signal).

This closes the best_time sweep that CLAUDE.md's Step 3 asks for: every published tree on the site has now been read once with this specific question in mind, not just the handful of headline genera (ginkgo, wisteria, sakura) that prompted the original pass. Diff touched only the `best_time` field in `data/cities/antwerp.json` and `data/cities/munich.json`, each file's existing single-file JSON formatting convention preserved. Site rebuilt (556 pages), all contracts validated, committed and pushed alongside the day's other work.

## 2026-07-29 — New collection: Trees Planted by Kings and Their Gardeners; BACKLOG.md's stale approval-gate status note corrected

Same session, after the Istanbul/Granada/Malaga/Athens photo-pin pass below turned up genuine dead ends across the top depth-priority cities. With rungs 3-5 given real, documented effort today and nothing left to try without new environment access, moved to PRODUCT_TODO.md's rung 7, item 4 (the collections programme), which explicitly allows drafting up to two per pass.

**Noticed first: `data/collections/BACKLOG.md` still said "ON HOLD per Hidde, 2026-07-19... never published without his approval"**, directly contradicting CLAUDE.md and SEO_GEO_BLUEPRINT.md's own recorded blueprint v1.3 (2026-07-27, "yes pas aan"): collections have published without an approval gate for two days, and PRODUCT_TODO.md item 4 (not this file) is the live queue a run actually draws from. This is exactly a "fact that does not hold up" in the project's own docs, so corrected the header to say plainly that it's superseded and point at the current policy, and refreshed the stale "Shipped" list (was still showing 2 of the 10 collections that actually exist). Left the rest of the file's research (search terms, GEO angles, coverage counts) as historical record rather than rewriting it.

**Drafted "Trees Planted by Kings and Their Gardeners"** (BACKLOG.md idea #2), the next-best-fit slate item after collections already shipped and "Trees older than 1000 years" stayed honestly blocked (still only 3 qualifying trees). A rough keyword scan across all 36 cities' stories for royal-adjacent words turned up roughly 55 candidates; dispatched a research pass to read every one's full story against the actual bar (planted by, for, or on the direct order of a named monarch or royal appointee, not merely standing in a royal park or mentioned near one) rather than trust the keyword match. **13 survived**: Kyoto's Emperor's Camphor (carried north and replanted by a retired emperor, 1160) and Emperor's Gift Cherry (given away by Emperor Komei), the Land Boat Pine (a shogun's own bonsai, potted decades before it ever touched ground), Sensoji's War Ginkgo (a shogun's legendary planted branch, hedged as legend since no document confirms it), the Shiba Toshogu Ginkgo and the Three Hundred Year Pine (both shogun plantings), Istanbul's Cypresses of Karacaahmet (Nurbanu Sultan's direct planting order with an assigned guard detail), both of Paris's Robinier plantings (Henri IV's herbalist and his son, royal botanist in turn, the same family across two generations), London's Sweet Chestnut of Greenwich Park (Charles II's own avenue-planting order), Milan's ginkgo pair (planted the year after Empress Maria Theresa founded the garden), Athens' Holm Oak of the National Garden (Queen Amalia's original planting scheme), and Dublin's Queen Victoria's Sequoia (planted by her own hand, 1861, the tree already carries her name).

**Roughly 40 candidates correctly rejected**, each for a real, checkable reason rather than a vibe: London's Royal Oak predates the royal park wall around it (the story's own words, "the wall came second"); the Evelyn Mulberry's own text says "historians doubt Peter planted anything here"; Vienna's Sisi-Platane is only named for a habit, not a planting; the Craigmillar Yews carry an unconfirmed local tradition about Mary Queen of Scots with no claim of who planted them; Nice's Villa Masséna palms were planted by a landscape architect for a Napoleonic-title prince, not reigning royalty; and roughly 35 more where a monarch's name appeared only for unrelated context (a war, a cemetery, a hunting ground, an institution's naming). Full per-tree reasoning is in the drafting agent's own report.

**No new superlatives were introduced.** Every strong claim in the collection ("oldest tree in Paris," "almost certainly the oldest parent and child trees in any European city," "oldest documented trees in Milan by a wide margin") is copied straight from that tree's own already-published, already-verified story text, checked against the source city file directly before this entry was written, not invented fresh for the collection (hard rule 8).

Wrote `data/collections/trees-planted-by-kings.json`, `status: published` per blueprint v1.3's no-approval regime. Site rebuilt: 556 pages, 11 public collections (up from 10), all contracts validated. Committed and pushed alongside the research-dead-ends entry below.

## 2026-07-29 — RBGE's Living Collection database tried for Edinburgh's two Botanics pins: genuinely tooling-blocked, not a content dead end

Same session, a small final check. A 2026-07-27 entry had flagged `data.rbge.org.uk`'s Living Collection database as "worth trying... in a future pass, not attempted this session" for tightening `edi_002` (Sweet Chestnut) and `edi_004` (Cedar of Lebanon), Edinburgh's two remaining Botanics approximate pins. Edinburgh sits at 10/10 approximate, the worst pin ratio on the site, so this was worth a real attempt rather than leaving the flag open indefinitely.

**Confirmed genuinely blocked by this session's tooling, not by content.** The site's search page (`data.rbge.org.uk/living/`) is a form with no discoverable action URL or field name in the rendered markdown (WebFetch strips form internals), and guessing common query-string patterns (`?taxon=`, `?query=`, `?name=`) all returned the same blank, unsubmitted form rather than results or even a clean 404 for a wrong parameter, consistent with a POST-only or session-token-gated form. This is the same shape of wall as Athens' JS-rendered GIS layer earlier today: a real, reachable site with real data behind it, but no way for this session's fetch tooling to actually submit the query. Worth a retry by a session that can inspect real page source or drive a form submission properly; not worth more blind parameter-guessing this session.

No data changed. Recording so a future session doesn't re-flag this as merely "not attempted" when it has, in fact, now been tried and specifically hit a tooling wall.

## 2026-07-29 — Lyon: the Cedar of Île Barbe pulled, unverifiable and possibly not open to the public (rung 3, touches hard rule 10)

Same session, following a Lyon photo/pin research pass (below) that flagged this entry's sourcing as thin. Checked both of lyo_007's own cited sources directly (lignedroite.fr, o2-saone.fr): neither actually names a cedar of Lebanon, or any tree species at all, on Île Barbe, despite the entry's confident claim that "visitors and local dendrological surveys single it out by name." Widened the search: the official DIREN Rhône-Alpes "site classé" heritage brochure for Île Barbe (a primary government source, fetched and read in full), several Lyon tourism guides, and the official lyon.fr page all describe the island in detail and none mentions a cedar anywhere. The DIREN brochure is explicit about the one relevant fact that does matter here: the island splits into a public south (a meadow "planté de majestueux platanes", plane trees) and a private, residential north where "les jolis jardins... se cachent derrière les murs... et ne se visitent pas" (the gardens hide behind walls and are not open to visits). If a cedar exists at all, the most likely place for it is exactly that private half.

This stacks two separate problems: an unverifiable species/existence claim (hard rule 2, never publish with false confidence what you cannot verify) and a real risk of directing readers to private, non-visitable land (hard rule 10, "when in doubt, leave it out," which explicitly outranks goal 1, goal 2 and speed). Rather than downgrade to merely `flagged`, pulled the tree entirely: `curation_status: flagged` was already set (for the age estimate, a much smaller issue) and had not caught this. Lyon drops from 10 to 9 trees, still comfortably above the 4-tree minimum for a page.

Added a `REMOVED_TREE_SLUGS` mechanism to `build_site.py`, alongside the existing `RENAMED_TREE_SLUGS`, so the tree's old URL (`/lyon/cedar-of-ile-barbe`) redirects to the Lyon city page instead of 404ing, in case it was ever indexed. This is a new, small, reversible piece of infrastructure, not a one-off hack: any future pull of a tree with no replacement can reuse it by adding one line.

Nothing here needs Hidde's input, this was a reversible, log-it-and-move-on call per the mandate, but flagging it prominently given hard rule 10 is the one rule that overrides everything else.

## 2026-07-28 — A site-wide best_time sweep: 15 genuine seasonal peaks added across 7 cities, after noticing the field is far more under-used than the photo/pin backlog

Same session, following the Istanbul pass below. Rung 5 names "seasonality where real" as part of what makes Amsterdam (the reference city) flawless, and after Amsterdam's and Lisbon's photo gaps turned out to be exhaustively dead-ended yet again, checked whether `best_time` itself was under-applied anywhere, a genuinely different kind of depth work from more photo/pin hunting. It was: scanning every published city's trees for a missing `best_time` field turned up a large candidate list (roughly 200 of 346 trees have none), most correctly so (evergreens, inconspicuous-flowering species, trees without a real standout moment, matching the rule's own Totteridge Yew example), but a genuine, well-documented handful had real peaks nobody had ever flagged. Added 15, each verified against a species/location-specific source rather than assumed from genus alone, and explicitly skipped candidates where sources disagreed or only gave Southern Hemisphere timing with no Iberian confirmation (Malaga's Yellow Poinciana, Cadiz's Floss Silk Tree, whose own municipal source says only "spring to autumn," too broad to be an honest signal).

**Added:**
- **Amsterdam `ams_008`, the Amstelkade Olive Willow**: May-June, fragrant yellow flowers (Wikipedia + multiple Dutch nursery sources agree).
- **Lisbon `lis_005` (Tipu, late winter into spring, per its own already-cited getlisbon.com source), `lis_006` (Coral Trees, June, matching the entry's own existing story text rather than a generic species search that disagreed), `lis_009` (Paineira, October, matching its own existing story).**
- **Kyoto `kyo_005`, `kyo_008`, `kyo_010`, all three weeping cherries**: this was the biggest real gap on the site, sakura season is the single most obvious "go now" signal Kyoto's pages could give and none of the three had it. Late March into early April, verified per-tree against temple/shrine-specific bloom-date sources (Maruyama Park, Daigo-ji, Kamigamo Shrine each independently confirm the same window).
- **New York `nyc_002` (Black Tupelo, late Sept-Oct scarlet, often the first tree in the Ramble to turn), `nyc_004` (Sassafras, October multicolour), `nyc_005` and `nyc_007` (both Tulip Trees, October golden yellow)**: all well-documented Northeast-US fall-color species, verified against arboretum/extension sources.
- **Malaga `mlg_003` (El Barrilito, Ceiba chodatii, February-May cream-white flowers, per a Malaga-specific garden blog), `mlg_004` (the Cortina del Muelle Ceiba speciosa, October-November, leafless pink bloom, per a different Malaga-specific source, notably NOT the same window as Lisbon's own paineira despite being a close relative, so not assumed across cities)**.
- **Porto `por_003`, the pōhutukawa**: May-July in mainland Portugal, deliberately checked against Portuguese sources rather than inferred from the Southern-Hemisphere "New Zealand Christmas tree" framing already in its own story, which would have given the wrong months entirely if translated naively.
- **Lyon `lyo_010`, the Cappadocian Maple**: October-November, reliable golden-yellow autumn colour, well documented for the species.
- **Nice `nce_010`, the Strawberry Tree of Château de Crémat**: October-December, when Arbutus unedo flowers and ripens the previous year's fruit at the same time, a genuinely distinctive dual display.
- **Prague `prg_010`, Neruda's Pear Tree**: April blossom, corroborated by the entry's own already-recorded 29 April 2023 photo of the tree in bloom.

**Explicitly checked and correctly left alone**: Istanbul (all planes, cypresses, stone pine and mastic tree, no distinctive moment among any of them), Rome, Barcelona, Paris, Tokyo and London (each fully scanned, remaining gaps are evergreens or non-standout species, consistent with the rule's own guidance against forcing one on every tree); Florence's Honey Locust (fall colour is real but no source gave a specific month, only generic "autumn", too weak to publish as a "go now" signal). Not yet scanned: roughly 18 remaining published cities (Antwerp, Athens, Berlin, Bologna, Brussels, Dublin, Edinburgh, Granada, Milan, Munich, Naples, Seville, Valencia, Venice, Verona, Vienna, Sintra, Palermo), worth a future pass with the same method, though a first read of their species lists suggests most are genuinely evergreen/non-standout (oaks, planes, cedars, cypresses, palms) rather than more Kyoto-sized misses.

Each addition rebuilt and validated individually before committing; diffs kept minimal by preserving each file's existing single-line JSON formatting for unrelated fields rather than letting Python's json.dump reformat the whole file.

## 2026-07-28 — A real broken photo link found and fixed (Madrid's Ahuehuete), then a site-wide URL-integrity check run for more of the same

Same session, continuing the approved-photo spot-check below after Wikimedia's image servers started 429-rate-limiting direct fetches. Trying to re-fetch Madrid's `mad_001` (The Ahuehuete of the Parterre) kept failing, and the failure turned out not to be the rate limit: Commons' own API, queried for the exact filename `Ahuehuete_porte.JPG`, reports its current path as `f/f2/`, while the entry's stored URL used `thumb/e/e4/...1024px-...`, and requesting that stored path directly (via WebFetch, a separate fetch path from this session's rate-limited `urllib`) returned a genuine HTTP 404, not a rate-limit response. **This was a real dead link on a currently `approved`, live photo, not a licensing or subject problem.**

Fetched the real `f/f2` path directly and viewed it before touching anything: it is unambiguously the same tree, a multi-trunked Montezuma Cypress with drooping foliage inside the same ornamental iron fence the story describes. Fixed the stored URL to the correct path (`https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f2/Ahuehuete_porte.JPG`), keeping the same license and attribution, which were never in question.

**Worth understanding why, since Wikimedia's own hash-based paths are supposed to be stable**: the most likely explanation is the file was renamed on Commons at some point after this entry's URL was recorded (a rename changes the hash, since it is derived from the filename), which is a normal, ordinary Commons event, not a sign this project fabricated the URL. Nothing in this project's own history explains it either way; recorded here rather than guessed at.

**Given a genuine broken link exists, ran a site-wide check for the same class of bug**: extracted every stored photo URL's hash-prefix path and compared it against Commons' API-reported current path for that same filename, city by city in alphabetical order. Got through Amsterdam alphabetically to roughly New York/Nice/Palermo (~100 of 177 total photo URLs, everything with a Commons-style path) before Wikimedia's API also started rate-limiting this session. **Two more real, confirmed dead links found in that batch, both fixed the same way (correct path found via the API, fetched and viewed directly before swapping, license/attribution untouched):**
- **`nyc_003`, New York's Camperdown Elm** (stored `thumb/e/e0/...`, real `2/20`). Viewed the corrected image: the tree's signature weeping, umbrella-shaped canopy inside a low iron fence, an exact match.
- **`nyc_005`, the Queens Giant** (stored `e/ef/...`, real `6/67`). Viewed the corrected image: a tall bare tree towering above its neighbours in a leaf-litter forest floor, consistent with NYC's tallest tree in Alley Pond Park.

Continued the same check further (Nice onward) with a longer per-request delay, then closed it out completely with a final pass that retries on HTTP 429 with exponential backoff (15s, 30s, ...) instead of giving up, rather than leaving a partial sweep behind. **That final pass got through all 177 of 177 Commons-hosted photo URLs on the site, no exceptions, and found 2 more genuine dead links beyond the three already fixed above:**
- **`bcn_002`, Barcelona's Carob of the Viaduct** (stored `thumb/e/e2/...`, real `0/0a`). Viewed the corrected image: the same 1904 stereoscopic photograph of a tree trunk framed between the viaduct's stone arches, an exact match.
- **`kyo_002`, Kyoto's Land Boat Pine** (stored `thumb/8/8e/...`, real `4/4f`). Viewed the corrected image: the same boat-shaped trained pine beside Kinkaku-ji's roofline, an exact match.

Both fixed the same way as the other three (correct path found via the API, fetched and viewed directly to confirm subject before swapping, license/attribution untouched).

**Final, complete tally for this pass: 177 of 177 Commons-hosted photo URLs checked (plus the 22 non-Wikimedia URLs, separately confirmed reachable via a plain HTTP HEAD request), 5 confirmed dead links found and fixed site-wide (Madrid's Ahuehuete, New York's Camperdown Elm, New York's Queens Giant, Barcelona's Carob of the Viaduct, Kyoto's Land Boat Pine), zero false positives.** Every mismatch the technique flagged was independently confirmed by fetching and viewing the corrected image before anything changed, not trusted on the API's word alone. This closes out the URL-integrity question raised by the original approved-photo spot-check below: the site's photo links are now confirmed complete and correct as of this session, not just a sample.

Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

**All 7 held up.** Craigmillar shows a large conifer at the castle's inner court matching the file's own "yew trees, inner courtyard" Commons categorisation; Berlin's Kaisereiche is an unmistakable mature oak at a street corner; Antwerp's Peace Tree is a plausible ~30-year sapling-grown tree at the Grote Markt; Rome's Leccio is a distinctive old gnarled evergreen oak right at the lake's edge exactly as the story describes, healthy-looking in frame; the Brussels pond shot correctly shows the Bois de la Cambre's lake and chalet, a legitimate ensemble/setting illustration even though the framing foliage in shot is more maple-shaped than oak or beech; Vienna's Hauptallee photo is a genuine avenue of mature deciduous trees (an odd red-painted trunk mark is the visual focus, unusual but not wrong for an ensemble-avenue claim); Tokyo's ginkgo is an unmistakable massive ginkgo with its own interpretive sign in frame. This is a small sample against 126 candidates, so it is reassurance, not full clearance: a future session with time to spare could keep working down the same list. Full list of the 126 (by city/tree id) is reproducible with the same one-line filter (approved photos whose notes lack a view-confirmation phrase) rather than needing to be copied here in full.

**Nice, flagged for its palm entries specifically re: red palm weevil.** `nce_008` (First Canary Island Date Palm of France, Parc Vigier) already hedges carefully, per an earlier fix, that it's the *acclimatisation act* and general palm collection being described, not a claim that the individual 1864 specimen survives today, so the entry was already immune to this risk. Found one alarming-sounding lead, "40 century-old palms decimated" at Parc Vigier, but traced it to a propalmes83.com article dated 27 November 2017, well outside this project's mid-2025+ news window; a 2025-dated stat elsewhere (only 15 public palms lost citywide this year versus 879 over the prior five) is actually a positive trend, not a new-damage signal. No Nice-specific 2025-2026 weevil damage confirmed at Parc Vigier. No change.

## 2026-07-28 — A full news sweep of all 36 published cities' flagship trees (rung 3, first time run at this scale): one real fix, several open threads, mostly clean

Same session, after the Amsterdam/Venice/Lisbon depth work below. Every below-floor city has been hunted for photos and pins many times over today (documented exhaustively elsewhere in this file), so rather than repeat that, checked something CLAUDE.md ranks above it: whether anything already published has gone wrong since it shipped. Storm damage, disease, felling and access closures are exactly the "something published is wrong" case rung 3 exists for, and no prior session had swept all 36 cities' trees for this at once (only Verona and Palermo had a dedicated fresh check today). Dispatched three parallel research agents (Iberia/France/Italy, Northern Europe/Ireland, Asia/Americas/Turkey) covering all ~340 trees against news since roughly mid-2025, then followed up myself on every lead with a real, un-dismissed signal, using a technique worth recording: Google News RSS (`news.google.com/rss/search?q=...`, stdlib `urllib`, no API key, no dependency) reliably surfaces relevant articles even after this session's WebSearch tool budget was exhausted; article text itself often still needs a direct `urllib` fetch with a browser User-Agent since many news sites (BBC, several UK regionals) return 403 to WebFetch specifically.

**The one real, confirmed problem, fixed:** `bcn_004`, Barcelona's Himalayan Cedar of the Labyrinth. The published `access` field said "Paid entry, free Wednesdays and Sundays" as if the park were open. It is not: Parc del Laberint d'Horta closed to the public in March 2025 for a EUR1.5m cypress-hedge and irrigation renovation (beteve.cat, 3Cat and El Punt Avui all reported this in March 2025, explicitly stating "el parc estarà tancat al públic", the whole park, not just the vegetal maze as an earlier same-day session entry on a different city guessed might be the case), extended a further year afterward for the new planting to root, with reopening due March 2027. A 6 December 2025 Ajuntament de Barcelona article ("Cypress trees now being planted at the Laberint d'Horta") confirms the works, and therefore the closure, were still active as of December 2025, safely inside this project's mid-2025-to-now window. The tree itself is not reported damaged, only inaccessible. Fixed both the `access` field (now states the closure and expected reopening plainly) and the story's closing sentence, which described admission pricing that no longer applies while the gate is shut. This is exactly the class of error the mandate cares about most: not a wrong fact about the tree, but a broken promise to the visitor who follows the page today.

**Open threads, real signal but not resolved either way, recorded so a future session does not have to re-discover them:**
- **Naples nap_008 (Pine of Posillipo, already an honestly-labelled 1995 replacement)**: a direct follow-up (Google News RSS, since this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted) confirms the Posillipo pine dieback is larger and more recent than first recorded: **la Repubblica reported 70 pines felled district-wide on 3 March 2026** ("sono pericolosi, e impossibile salvarli"), Il Mattino reported another single felling on 27 February 2026 with residents complaining of no maintenance, and new pines were being replanted at Parco Virgiliano by 28 April 2026. Every article found names Via Manzoni or Parco Virgiliano specifically, not Via Minucio Felice near Chiesa di Sant'Antonio, where this entry's own tree stands, and none could be fetched in full text (Il Mattino and la Repubblica both block this session's fetch tooling), so the specific specimen's status could not be confirmed either way. Given the scale (70+ trees in one district in one month) this is a real, live, unresolved risk, not a settled dead end; worth a direct on-the-ground check or a query against Naples' own tree registry before the next Naples pass, rather than more news searching with the same tooling.
- **Naples nap_009 (Plane Tree of the Villa Comunale)**: two articles (premiogreencare.org "Alberi monumentali a rischio nella Villa Comunale di Napoli"; ilmattino.it on a 5-month closure for "analisi delle piante") suggest real risk/closure activity at this garden, but neither source's date or scope (which specific trees) could be confirmed this pass.
- **Rome rom_006 (Leccio of the Lake, Villa Borghese)**: romatoday.it reports "decine di lecci" (dozens of holm oaks) at Villa Borghese with beetle-hollowed trunks, a real regional risk to this entry's own species and park, not confirmed as naming this specific tree.
- **Lisbon lis_002 (Ajuda Dragon Tree)**: Portugal's Museu Nacional de História Natural e da Ciência reported Jardim Botânico da Ajuda "badly affected" by storm Kristin (28 January 2026), without naming which species were hit; this is the garden's own flagship tree, so worth a direct follow-up.
- **Barcelona bcn_002 (Carob of the Viaduct)** and other Barcelona/Valencia items: the research agents ran out of session search budget before finishing every tree; see their full per-city notes in the researching agents' own transcripts if a future session wants the exhaustive list of what was and wasn't checked.
- **Valencia**: largely unresearched this pass (budget exhausted almost immediately). One old (September 2022, well outside this project's mid-2025+ window) article mentions "el Ficus del Parterre que cayó" (a fig at the Parterre garden that fell) in passing, in the same garden as this project's flagship `vlc_001` El Titan, but describes a different, smaller specimen inspected in the same visit and predates the window regardless; not treated as current. Valencia's other 9 trees need a dedicated fresh pass, ideally after storms Martinho (March 2025) and the DANA aftermath specifically, given the city's own council review of its monumental-tree catalogue "after the fall of numerous specimens."
- **Dublin, Berlin, Munich, Prague, Nice**: incompletely searched (budget exhausted mid-city); no specific bad news surfaced for any named tree in what was checked, but these should not be read as a clean bill of health, only as "checked and found nothing so far." Storm Éowyn (Ireland/UK, January 2025) and general regional storm activity make Dublin in particular worth a dedicated re-check.

**Checked and found genuinely clean, worth recording so it isn't re-run identically**: Amsterdam, Antwerp, Athens (except a flagged-but-untried canker-stain check on its plane trees), Brussels, Edinburgh's Cedar of Lebanon and Sweet Chestnut specifically (see below), London, Madrid, Granada, Seville, Cadiz, Tokyo, Kyoto, Istanbul, New York, Paris, Lyon (6 of 10 trees; 4 unchecked), Florence, Milan (partial), Bologna (partial), Porto, Sintra (no named tree confirmed hit despite heavy storm losses at Pena/Monserrate; a detailed loss inventory for the January 2026 storm names no araucaria, pohutukawa or "walking tree" among the fallen).

**Edinburgh, checked in direct follow-up rather than left as a flagged risk**: a same-day sub-agent flagged that Storm Éowyn (24 January 2025) hit the Royal Botanic Garden Edinburgh hard and wondered whether `edi_002` (Sweet Chestnut of the Botanics) or `edi_004` (Cedar of Lebanon, standing near the Glasshouses, which lost "100 panes of glass" in the same storm) might be among the losses. Fetched RBGE's own storm statement directly (via deadlinenews.co.uk, which quotes it in full): "15 trees have been lost, either uprooted or damaged beyond recovery. This includes the Garden's tallest tree, the 29-metre cedrus deodara, planted in 1859... A further 27 trees have been heavily damaged." No species besides the deodar is named in RBGE's own statement or in the BBC/Scotsman/STV coverage checked. One sub-agent's report of "centuries-old horse chestnut trees felled" turned out, on reading the actual source, to be a Facebook commenter's reminiscence about an unrelated 1990s storm in Buckinghamshire, not RBGE Edinburgh at all; caught and correctly not treated as evidence. No further news found tying either the Cedar of Lebanon or the Sweet Chestnut to any 2025-2026 loss. Absence of a specific mention is not proof of safety given "15 lost, 27 damaged" were never itemised by species, but with the storm's own most newsworthy casualty (the tallest tree) being a different species and no press anywhere naming either of these two, there is no positive evidence to act on. Left unchanged; worth a direct email or on-site check by a future session rather than more searching.

**Nice's three palm entries**: checked specifically for red palm weevil (a well-documented threat across the Côte d'Azur), both via WebSearch (before budget ran out) and Google News RSS in French. Real registered trees turned up at several of these exact locations, but none usefully corrects this project's own entries:

- **A real Phoenix canariensis (Canary Island Palm) is registered at "Isola di San Servolo" with exact coordinates** (RAMI #3379, 45.419726, 12.355279), but this project's `ven_006` is a different species (Olea europaea, an olive), so this is a different tree on the same small island, not a match. - **Two "Bagolaro" (Celtis australis) records exist in Venezia comune, at Sant'Erasmo and Piazzale Roma**, neither matching `ven_008`'s own location (Villa Groggia, Cannaregio); correctly left unmatched. - Zero results at all for "Groggia" and "Marghera" as keywords (`ven_008` and `ven_010`).
- No data changed. Venice stays 10/10 approximate, 4/10 photos. RAMI is now confirmed genuinely exhausted for Venice's specific entries (real coverage exists in the area, just not for these named trees with usable coordinates); not worth re-trying without a new angle.

**A reusable capability worth recording for future sessions**: this session confirmed image files CAN be fetched (`urllib`) and viewed directly (`Read` tool) this time, unlike the gap a recent session flagged. Also wrote and tested a small pure-Python, stdlib-only PDF parser (regex + `zlib`, no new dependency, same spirit as the earlier from-scratch OLE2/BIFF8 Excel parser for MASAF) that successfully downloads Istanbul's İBB "Anıt Ağaç Rota" Google Drive PDF brochures (flagged as untried in an earlier 2026-07-28 entry, file IDs already recorded there) and extracts embedded JPEG images directly from the raw PDF bytes. Tried on the Gülhane brochure (covers `ist_004`): 16 real photographs extracted and one viewed directly, a lit night photo of a large plane tree. **Still not usable as a photo source**: no license or copyright statement could be found anywhere in the PDF (its text layer uses a custom/embedded font encoding this session's regex-based extractor cannot decode into readable characters, so a copyright page, if present, could not be read), and İBB's own web properties have already been confirmed elsewhere to carry no reachable open-licence statement. Recording the *capability* (PDF image extraction now works without any new dependency) separately from the *result* (still blocked on licence, same as every other İBB source), so a future session with better font-decoding doesn't have to rediscover that the download itself works.

**Genuine dead end, but with new information worth recording**: archief.amsterdam now returns HTTP 403 to both direct fetch and its newer JS-rendered search UI (a bot-block, confirming and hardening the "beeldbank.amsterdam.nl no longer resolves" finding from an earlier 2026-07-28 session), and Europeana's search UI also returns HTTP 403 to both direct query and its demo API key. ## 2026-07-28 — Second Istanbul pin resolved: `ist_001`, the 1,382-year Bahçeköy plane, upgraded to confirmed

Follow-up in the same session as the entry below. The prior note on `ist_001` (Istanbul's oldest tree) held it at approximate because the matched registry record's species field (Platanus acerifolia) contradicted the sourced species (Platanus orientalis). Tested that directly rather than leaving it asserted: pulled every Platanus record the same national registry carries for all of Sarıyer district (40 trees total). This record is the single oldest of the 40 by nearly 600 years (next is 823), and several other records the registry also labels "Londra Çınarı/Platanus acerifolia" carry ages of 816 and 579 years, themselves taxonomically impossible for a true London Plane hybrid (the cross only originated around 1670, capping a real specimen near 350 years). That confirms the registry's Turkish-name-to-Latin-name pairing is unreliable site-wide for old plane trees, not uniquely wrong for this one. Combined with independent non-registry news sourcing already matching this record's age closely and its coordinates already sitting at the stored pin, upgraded to confirmed; the species-label caveat stays recorded in the tree's own notes rather than resolved. Istanbul now has 3 approximate pins left (ist_004, ist_008, ist_009); tried to resolve all three further this pass (Fatih age/diameter query for ist_004's Taşlı Çınar, an Istanbul-wide Pistacia lentiscus search for ist_008's Florya Mastic Tree) and found no close-enough matches, so both stay approximate and undisturbed.

New session. Ran `python3 scripts/visitors.py`: 75 visits, 132 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17). No reader submissions. Site healthy (555 pages). **A fresh rung-3 check, done by actual web search rather than trusting old log entries**: searched for Verona's Cedar of Piazza Bra (flagged in a 2026-07-26 entry as "worth a re-check given the tree's own history of risk") and for Palermo's diseased Piazza Marina fig. The Verona cedar is confirmed still stable: a technical survey completed shortly before Ferragosto 2025 and re-verified in October 2025 found the cabling/anchor system holding with no signs of criticality, matching the site's existing text exactly. Barney the Plane's own notes had explicitly flagged this exact gap since 2026-07-22 ("Story still leans general rather than tree-specific and runs under the usual word count; worth a fuller rewrite in a future pass") and it had gone unaddressed since.

Rewrote all 9, grounded entirely in facts already present in each entry's own `verified_sources` and `notes` fields (no new claims, nothing requiring new sourcing): the Barney rewrite in particular pulls directly from its own notes (the 2026 EXIF-to-plaque coordinate match) since the story previously never mentioned it.
