## 2026-07-28 — A systematic geocode audit across all 346 trees catches two real errors in Edinburgh (2.2km and 1.1km off), on top of Granada, Dublin and Brussels

Same session as the entries below. The Brussels Kasterlinde fix (a 1.3km location error found by geocoding a street name directly) suggested the same might be lurking elsewhere: an honestly-labelled `approximate` pin and a *wrong* pin look identical in the data unless someone actually checks the address against a real geocode. Wrote `scripts/geocode_audit.py` (stdlib `urllib` only, one Nominatim request per second per its usage policy, not a build step, a one-off research aid) to fetch every one of the 346 trees' own stored `address` field through Nominatim and flag any result more than 800m from the stored coordinate. Many addresses come back "no result" (Nominatim's full-text geocoder doesn't parse every descriptive address string), which is expected and not itself a problem; the useful signal is the entries that DO resolve, cleanly, to somewhere else.

- **Edinburgh's Craigmillar Castle Yews (edi_001): a 2.2km error, fixed.** The stored coordinate did not point at Craigmillar Castle at all. Confirmed two independent ways before touching anything: Nominatim's own geocode of "Craigmillar Castle Road, Bingham, Craigmillar" resolves to this entry's own cited postcode (EH16 4SY) at (55.9256, -3.1408), and Wikipedia's stated coordinate for the castle is (55.9259, -3.1406), the two agreeing within 40m of each other. Moved the pin there. This is Edinburgh's flagship entry and the city's own `question_answer` field names it as the city's oldest documented tree; it had been sending anyone who followed the map 2.2km into the wrong part of the city since this page went live.
- **Edinburgh's Cammo Ash (edi_003): a 1.1km error, fixed.** Same pattern: Nominatim's geocode of "Cammo Estate, Cammo Walk" and an independent park-directory listing (55.9564, -3.3215, OS grid NT175745, postcode EH4 8AW) agree within 150m of each other, and neither is anywhere near the stored pin. Moved it into the actual 85-acre reserve.
- Both stay `location_precision: approximate` (neither source surveys the specific tree, only the castle/estate they stand in), but both are now honestly approximate rather than confidently wrong, the exact distinction hard rule 2 exists to protect.
- **The other 8 Edinburgh entries, already worked hard across many earlier 2026-07-28 passes, check out fine**: edi_007 and edi_010 (recently tightened to church geocodes) sit within 5m and 1m of their own address's Nominatim result; edi_009 (Hermitage of Braid, an ensemble entry over a large reserve) is 525m off but that is expected for a whole-reserve claim, not a specific-point error; the remaining five return "no result" (institutional addresses too specific for Nominatim's text search) rather than a wrong match.
- **The audit finished: 12 of 346 trees flagged over 800m, verified one by one rather than trusted blind (the postcode in the query string turned out to trip Nominatim up on several Spanish addresses, so every flag got a clean second geocode plus an independent source before any pin moved).** 8 were real errors, now fixed:
  - **Edinburgh's Craigmillar Castle Yews and Cammo Ash** (2177m and 1060m), detailed above.
  - **Lyon's Cedar of Île Barbe (lyo_007, ~2km)**: the stored pin wasn't on the island at all; confirmed against both Nominatim's geocode of an amenity inside the garden and Wikipedia's own coordinate for Île Barbe, which agree within 250m of each other.
  - **Lyon's Oak Collection of Parc de la Cerisaie (lyo_008, ~1km)**: confirmed via two independent geocodes (the park's own name, and Rue Chazière) agreeing within 100m.
  - **Madrid's Stone Pine of the Priest's House (mad_010, ~1.4km)**: the stored pin wasn't inside El Capricho; moved to Nominatim's geocode of the park's official name, "Jardin historico El Capricho de la Alameda Osuna".
  - **Valencia's Ficus of the Countess of Ripalda (vlc_004, ~1.2km)**: same latitude, wrong longitude in the stored pin, a likely old data-entry slip; fixed, and the entry's own `neighbourhood` field was also wrong (said Extramurs; a web search independently confirms the plaza sits in Exposicio, El Pla del Real) and corrected alongside it.
  - **Nice's First Canary Island Date Palm (nce_008, ~1km)**: the stored pin sat inland of Parc Vigier, which is actually right at the port; fixed to Nominatim's geocode of both the park and its street.
  - **London's Mulberry of Charlton House (lon_006, ~1055m)**: confirmed against a clean Nominatim geocode of Charlton House itself. This entry also had an empty `verified_sources` array (a real gap, not just imprecision) with a note saying sources needed backfilling; found and added two (Morus Londinium, London Remembers) while fixing the pin.
  - The other 4 (Sintra's Pohutukawa of Monserrate at a reported 6684m, Malaga's Native Oak and Rare Cassia of Malaga Park at 860m/851m, Kyoto's Emperor's Gift Cherry at 850m) are **false positives**: a clean, targeted re-geocode of each (without the postcode, which is what confused the automated pass) landed within 10-170m of the stored coordinate every time, in Malaga's and Kyoto's case exactly matching pins this project had already carefully sourced from a specific OSM node. No change needed; recorded here so a future pass doesn't re-flag them.
- **Lesson for reusing `geocode_audit.py`**: query the address without the trailing postal code, or verify every flag by hand with a second, cleaner query before touching data. It's a lead generator, not a source; several of its raw flags this pass would have been wrong fixes if trusted directly.
- **Follow-up pass, `scripts/geocode_audit2.py`**: for the roughly 230 trees whose full address didn't geocode at all, tried a lighter landmark-style query (tree name + city) instead. Surfaced two more genuine wins and confirmed the technique's own failure mode: **London's Fulham Palace Oak (lon_007) was about 1km from the actual palace**, fixed and confirmed via two independent Nominatim hits, one matching this entry's own postcode exactly. The other two flags over 800m were both name collisions, not errors: Valencia's "Los Tres Amigos" matched an unrelated same-named place 122km away, and London's "Royal Oak" (an extremely common pub/place name in England) matched a different Royal Oak 10.6km off; that tree's real coordinate already rests on a specific, cited Woodland Trust Ancient Tree Inventory record (ID 40404), not a name search, so nothing there needed touching. Also found and closed a separate, real gap while in London's file: **two more trees (Cheapside Plane lon_003, Evelyn Mulberry lon_010) had empty `verified_sources` arrays** despite their own notes flagging the gap; backfilled both with two independent sources each, and while checking the Evelyn Mulberry's sources, found and fixed a third real pin error (roughly 700m, Sayes Court Street). A site-wide grep confirms zero trees now have an empty `verified_sources` array.
- **Running total for this session's location-error hunt: 10 real fixes across 7 cities** (Brussels, Edinburgh x2, Lyon x2, Madrid, Valencia, Nice, London x2), all independently confirmed before touching data, none forced.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Granada's palm species question resolved (not an error); Dublin's Holm Oak Avenue pin corrected via OSM's named-way geometry; Istanbul's Taşlı Çınar re-checked, stays a dead end

New session. Ran `python3 scripts/visitors.py` first: 75 visits, 130 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17). No reader submissions (CSV fetched fresh, header row only). Site healthy at session start (555 pages, all contracts validated), nothing published found wrong. Depth-exit-test bar re-checked directly: still 5/8 (Rome, Tokyo, Paris, London, Barcelona pass; Amsterdam 7/10 photos, Lisbon 6/10, Istanbul 2/10+5 approximate remain short). Read this file and LOG.md in full: every below-floor city has multiple 2026-07-28 entries already, real techniques tried and real dead ends recorded across Amsterdam, Lisbon, Istanbul, Rome, Naples, Valencia, Lyon, Granada, Verona, Brussels, New York, Dublin, Malaga, Porto, Edinburgh, Venice, Bologna, Madrid, Antwerp, Milan, Athens, Florence, Seville and more. PRODUCT_TODO.md re-checked: fully done or honestly blocked (item 4's 1000+-year-tree slate still stuck at 3 site-wide by direct script count: London's Totteridge Yew, Istanbul's Bahçeköy plane, Kyoto's Heian Cedar; items 1 and 2c are session-reserved). Rather than re-run documented dead ends, hunted specific open threads this file itself had flagged as unresolved rather than exhausted.

- **Granada gra_004 (The Palms of the Archbishop's Palace): the flagged species discrepancy is resolved, not an error.** An earlier pass this file flagged Granada's municipal park register (granada.org) as listing Plaza de Gracia's palms as Phoenix canariensis, apparently conflicting with this entry's Washingtonia robusta. Fetched the register directly: it lists 11 Phoenix canariensis within the *public park* itself, a straightforward tree inventory of the plaza's own 80 street/park trees. This entry's four Washingtonia robusta stand next door, inside the private garden of the building bordering the square, a separate planting the municipal park inventory was never going to include. Identified that building properly while checking: it is the Palacio de los Yanguas (architect Fernando Wilhelmi, built 1920-1925 for the family of the Marquis of Casablanca), now the seat of Granada's Diocesan Curia, which is why it is colloquially called the Archbishop's palace even though it was never built as the Archbishop's actual residence (that is a different building, on Plaza de Alonso Cano). Corroborated independently via degranada.net, resolving the entry's earlier single-source flag (previously only wastemagazine.es). Pin tightened about 90m to the Palacio de los Yanguas building point in Andalusia's official heritage register (IAPH Guia Digital, entry i21771, mirrored on Wikidata Q97604286); stays `approximate`, a building point rather than a survey of the palms themselves. Access note sharpened per hard rule 10: the palace's own garden is not open to the public, so the honest framing is viewed from the plaza, not entered, same pattern as Verona's Cedar of Villa Bersani. Story rewritten with the corrected building identity and history, still 150-250 words, no banned words or em dashes (checked directly).
- **Dublin dub_008 (The Holm Oak Avenue): a real pin correction using OpenStreetMap's actual mapped path, not a category search.** A 2026-07-27 entry in this file had found a promising Holm Oak/Pine cluster on "All Saints Road" about 650m from this entry's pin, but left it unresolved: "not confirmably the same named avenue rather than an unrelated modern street-planting scheme." Found the missing piece: the European Garden Heritage Network's own account of St Anne's Park (eghn.org) explicitly states that Main Avenue itself, not the separate All Saints Avenue elsewhere in the same park, is "lined with Austrian Pine and Holm Oak," directly confirming this entry's own subject. Queried OpenStreetMap's Overpass API for every named avenue inside the park and got Main Avenue back as an actual surveyed path (several nodes running east-west around 53.372N, between -6.178 and -6.194). The stored pin already sat close to this path by coincidence; nudged about 45m onto the path's own geometry at the matching longitude, replaced the address's confusing "near All Saints Road" (which belongs to that separate, real, but different avenue) with "Main Avenue, St Anne's Park," and upgraded `location_precision` to `confirmed`, since the pin now sits on the actual named, sourced avenue the entry describes rather than an estimate. This also confirms the 2026-07-27 pass's OSM candidate was a real thing (All Saints Avenue genuinely exists and is genuinely holm-oak-lined) without being the tree this entry is about, so a future pass doesn't need to re-litigate it as unexplained.
- **Brussels bru_002 (The Kasterlinde): a real 1.3km location error found and fixed, not just a precision refinement.** While checking whether the official Flemish heritage inventory (inventaris.onroerenderfgoed.be/erfgoedobjecten/302035, already cited for this entry's photo) exposed a GPS coordinate, geocoded the street it names (Kasterlindenstraat) directly and found it sits nowhere near this entry's stored pin: about 1.3km away, near the actual Berchem-Sainte-Agathe/Molenbeek-Saint-Jean/Dilbeek boundary. Triangulated three independent ways before trusting it: Kasterlindenstraat's own OSM geocode, the neighbouring Kerselaarstraat's, and a direct name search for "In De Linde," which turned up a real cafe at 177 Kasterlindenstraat, the exact landmark this entry's own story already names ("A street and a nearby cafe, In de Linde, both take their name from it"). All three converge on the same spot. Moved the pin there; stays `approximate` since it's anchored to the named cafe rather than a survey of the trunk, but this is a genuine correction of a wrong location, not a tightening of an honest approximation, the kind of error hard rule 2 exists to catch. The heritage inventory's own precise coordinate and Brussels' remarkable-tree register (sites.heritage.brussels) both stayed out of reach (no exposed GPS field, 403 respectively), so this rests on landmark triangulation, recorded honestly in the entry's own notes. Worth a spot-check of other entries whose stored coordinates were set before this project started geocoding street names directly; this one had gone unnoticed since Brussels first went live.
- **Istanbul ist_004 (The Taşlı Çınar): re-checked with a fresh angle, stays a genuine dead end.** Queried iNaturalist's API directly for Platanus orientalis near the pin (rather than the general web/Commons searches prior passes used). Found one CC-BY observation with good positional accuracy (11m) about 280m away, fetched and viewed the photo directly (a large plane behind a municipal culture-department protective fence, consistent with a monument tree under conservation) but the observation sits inside the İstanbul Archaeology Museum's own courtyard (tagged captive/cultivated), not the public street corner outside Gülhane Park's Alemdar gate this entry's own sources place the tree at. Geographically close but institutionally a different setting (a museum's admission-controlled garden versus a free public street corner), so left unconfirmed rather than guessed, matching the same caution a 2026-07-28 pass already recorded for this exact tree. A second candidate (500m positional accuracy) was too imprecise to use. The İBB PDF brochures this file flagged as a genuine unexhausted lead (covering ist_004, ist_007, ist_009) were retried via WebFetch, which can now reach Google Drive's redirect chain; both tried came back over WebFetch's 10MB cap, the same wall a 2026-07-28 pass already hit with different tooling. Confirms rather than changes Istanbul's count (2/10 photos, 5/10 approximate).
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Sintra photo hunt: genuine, exhaustive dead end on 2 of its 3 remaining gaps, real leads closed properly

Same session, right after Cadiz above. Sintra shipped today at 2/5 photos (40%), also below floor. Hunted its three missing photos (Fern Cork Oak of Quinta do Relógio, Plane Tree of Parque da Liberdade, Pohutukawa of Monserrate) across Wikimedia Commons, iNaturalist, Flickr and Openverse, in English and Portuguese search terms, across two agent passes (the second specifically finishing two leads the first flagged as unexhausted rather than accepting a partial search).

- **sin_002, the Fern Cork Oak ("Sobreira dos Fetos"): genuine dead end.** Confirmed real and findable under its Portuguese name (recent JN coverage of tourist vandalism), but its only identifiable photo (Flickr, "SOBREIRA DOS FETOS - SINTRA" by Jobarque) is all-rights-reserved. The one CC-BY iNaturalist Quercus suber candidate near the coordinates turned out, per the observer's own notes, to be 3.7km away on a forest logging road, a different tree. Stays `missing`.
- **sin_003, the Plane Tree of Parque da Liberdade: genuine dead end, thoroughly checked.** Wikimedia Commons' "Parque da Liberdade" category (47 files) was gone through in full across both passes, every file viewed directly, not sampled. Two files (`Parque_da_Liberdade_Sintra_28.jpg`, `_32.jpg`, both DiogoBaptista, CC BY-SA 4.0) show a large pale-barked broadleaf tree above the skating rink amphitheater steps, a plausible sighting, but I fetched and viewed the sharper of the two myself and the tree is too distant and partially obscured to confirm Platanus's mottled bark or palmate leaf shape with confidence. A near-miss file, "Plátano do Duche," was checked and correctly excluded: its own plaque confirms it's a different classified grove of 12 planes at Volta do Duche, elsewhere in Sintra. Stays `missing`.
- **sin_005, the Pohutukawa of Monserrate: genuine dead end, thoroughly checked.** The full ~20-image Vitor Oliveira/Portuguese_eyes "Parque de Monserrate" CC BY-SA 2.0 series was viewed image by image across both passes. Two images show a multi-trunked, shrub-like tree on open lawn, but with no red flower or silvery leaf underside visible to distinguish Metrosideros excelsa from Arbutus unedo (strawberry tree), also documented in this same garden with the same growth habit; correctly left unconfirmed rather than guessed between two candidate species. Openverse and iNaturalist searches for the species near Monserrate returned only Azores/Madeira results or CC-BY-NC/unlicensed observations. Stays `missing`.
- No data changed; recording this so a future session doesn't re-run the same two full Commons categories expecting a different result. iNaturalist (not yet tried specifically for the Parque da Liberdade plane or Monserrate pohutukawa beyond a radius search) remains a genuinely different, untried angle for a future pass.
- No site rebuild needed (no tree data changed); this entry alone was pushed with the next commit.

## 2026-07-28 — Cadiz photo hunt: 3 of its 4 missing photos found and viewed directly, clears the 80% floor

Same session, right after the city-trips collection above. Cadiz shipped today at 1/5 photos (20%), the weakest of the three brand-new cities and well under CLAUDE.md's photo target. A general-purpose research agent hunted its four missing photos (all four remaining trees stand in Parque Genoves: the dragon tree, the pohutukawa, the ombu, the floss silk tree) across Wikimedia Commons, iNaturalist, Flickr and Openverse, filtered strictly to CC0/CC-BY/CC-BY-SA.

- **3 found, all from the same iNaturalist photographer's single 2023-06-06 visit to the park (Bruno Peixoto, CC BY 4.0 throughout)**, and all three fetched and viewed directly by this session (not approved on caption alone) before adding:
  - **cad_002, the Dragon Tree**: viewed directly, shows a large multi-trunk dragon tree beside a children's playground with a domed gazebo behind it, both identifiable Parque Genoves features. Worth naming: the same iNaturalist observation carried an automated field mislabelling it "Drago del Obispado" (a different, older dragon tree at the Bishop's Palace elsewhere in the old town); the photo's own visual content, playground and gazebo don't belong to a palace courtyard, is what settled it, not the label. A real case for "view the image, don't trust the caption."
  - **cad_003, the Pohutukawa**: the strongest of the three. Viewed directly, and the tree's own interpretive sign is legible in the photo: "Arbol de hierro / Metrosideros tomentosa / Myrtaceae / Nueva Zelanda", an exact species and common-name match confirmed on sight.
  - **cad_005, the Floss Silk Tree**: viewed directly, shows the swollen, bottle-shaped, lightly thorned trunk characteristic of Ceiba speciosa in a raised planter among palms. Weaker evidence than the other two, its place_guess reads only "Cadiz," not the park specifically, but the coordinates sit about 250m from the confirmed dragon tree and pohutukawa observations, same photographer, same day, and the species' distinctive trunk shape makes a different specimen elsewhere in the city unlikely. Approved with that caveat recorded plainly in the tree's own notes field, not smoothed over.
- **cad_004, the Ombu: genuinely not found.** Checked Wikimedia Commons (Phytolacca dioica category, nothing in Cadiz), iNaturalist (filtered search returned zero within a 10km radius of the park; an unfiltered search found real observations but every one is CC-BY-NC or unlicensed), Openverse and Flickr. Stays `missing` rather than stretching a non-commercial candidate.

New session. Ran `python3 scripts/visitors.py` first: 70 visits, 105 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17), plus 5 so far today. No reader submissions (CSV fetched fresh via WebFetch through a redirect, header row only). Site healthy at session start: build clean (554 pages), nothing published found wrong.

**State check before picking work**: the depth-exit-test bar is unchanged at 5/8 (Rome, Tokyo, Paris, London, Barcelona pass; Amsterdam 7/10 photos, Lisbon 6/10, Istanbul 2/10+5 approximate all remain short). Checked how exhaustively today's many prior sessions had already worked every below-floor city: every single one (Naples, Istanbul, Valencia, Lyon, Granada, Verona, Brussels, New York, Dublin, Malaga, Porto, Edinburgh, Venice, Bologna, Madrid, Antwerp, Milan, Athens, and more) already has 10+ mentions in today's CURATION.md entries, real techniques tried and real dead ends recorded. Re-running any of it would be exactly the looping CLAUDE.md warns against. PRODUCT_TODO.md is fully done or honestly blocked (item 4's "trees older than 1000 years" still stuck at 3 site-wide).

**Found real, unstarted work instead**: since Sintra went live (see entry below), Palermo, Cadiz and Sintra, the three cities Hidde pre-approved specifically to complete "the 10 best tree city trips of Europe" (his direction, BACKLOG.md 2026-07-27, research and draft ranking recorded in this file's 2026-07-27 entry), are all live. Three separate 2026-07-28 entries below all flagged the same next step, unstarted: "the collection page itself and its walking routes still need to be built." Built it this pass.

**The routing question, resolved without a new dependency.** Hidde's own publication bar from the 2026-07-27 session: "an entry is only done when its route is actually plotted over streets with start/end and elevation." Rather than add a routing API (a new third-party dependency, hard rule 5, needing his yes) or fabricate precision this project explicitly forbids, reused the site's own existing walk planner: every city page already computes an honest walkable cluster (`plan_walking_route()` in `scripts/build_site.py`, nearest-neighbour growth from every possible start, capped at a 6 km real-street-adjusted budget, 1.35x detour factor, same function already live and trusted on all 36 city pages) and hands real turn-by-turn routing to the visitor's own phone via the same Google Maps deep link used everywhere else on the site. Ran that exact algorithm against each of the eleven cities' full tree sets (imported nothing new, copied the function's logic directly) to get real, honest numbers rather than guessed ones:
- Palermo: 5/8 trees, 5.5 km, 73 min (flat)
- Seville: 9/10 trees, 4.3 km, 58 min (flat)
- Cadiz: 5/5 trees, 1.0 km, 13 min (flat, shortest on the list)
- Lisbon: 5/10 trees, 3.5 km, 46 min
- Sintra: 5/5 trees, 5.7 km, 76 min (genuinely hilly, Serra de Sintra terrain the flat number doesn't show)
- Porto: 10/10 trees, 2.6 km, 34 min (best tree-per-effort ratio on the list)
- Athens: 5/10 trees, 3.8 km, 51 min (plus a real climb up Filopappou Hill at the end)
- Prague: 7/10 trees, 4.6 km, 62 min
- Rome: 7/10 trees, 5.2 km, 69 min (genuinely hilly, climbs the Gianicolo to reach the Garibaldi Mausoleum cedar, matching Hidde's own "Gianicolo climb stated honestly" note)
- Milan: 7/10 trees, 5.0 km, 67 min (flat, as Hidde's session already flagged)
- Granada: 10/10 trees, 5.2 km, 69 min by distance, but flagged honestly in its entry that the flat number understates the real climb since most of these ten sit within or below the hilltop Alhambra and Generalife grounds

**A judgement call worth naming, since it departs from the literal city-count**: Hidde's own ranking treated "Lisbon+Sintra" as one combined trip, position 4 of 10. Contract D's entry structure is per-city, so this ships as two consecutive city sections rather than one merged one; the collection's intro states plainly that eleven cities carry ten rankings and that the two are meant as a paired day-trip a short train apart, so nobody reads it as a miscount.

**A structural choice for entry format, also worth naming**: rather than one entry per tree (as every other collection on the site does, and Contract D's own "2-3 sentences" spec assumes), this collection carries one entry per city, its flagship tree linked, with the whole walk (which other trees, how far, how long, real hills where they exist) folded into that one note. A per-tree structure here would have meant roughly 70 entries, most repeating the same walk-logistics sentence with the city name swapped, exactly the "repeated boilerplate phrases inside loops" PRODUCT_TODO.md's own human-first check warns against. One rich paragraph per trip reads better and stays honest; flagging the deviation from the contract's literal per-entry length guidance since it was a judgement call, not an oversight.

**Every fact re-checked against its own tree's already-verified story before use**, not re-derived: Palermo's "reportedly Europe's widest tree crown" and Cadiz's "reported as the oldest documented" pohutukawa both reuse claims this project already softened and sourced when those cities shipped, nothing new asserted. One correction made while checking: an early draft called Sintra's Walking Tree of Pena a "Pacific cedar"; its own story says western red cedar, fixed before publishing.

**Flagship tree chosen per city** (favouring an already-photographed, most-recognisable specimen that also falls inside the computed walk cluster): Palermo's Orto Botanico fig, Seville's Real Alcázar ginkgos, Cadiz's Alameda fig, Lisbon's Largo do Limoeiro ombú, Sintra's Monserrate araucaria, Porto's Palácio de Cristal cedar, Athens's Sacred Olive of the Acropolis, Prague's Beethoven Plane, Rome's Twin Planes of the Eleven Fountains, Milan's Parco Sempione walnut, Granada's Cypress of San Juan de la Cruz.

## 2026-07-28 — Sintra: 36th city live, 5 trees, the last of three cities pre-approved to complete the Europe city-trips collection

With today's depth ladder still exhaustively documented as dead for Amsterdam, Lisbon and Istanbul (many sessions today, all techniques tried, re-confirmed at session start rather than assumed) and PRODUCT_TODO.md fully done or honestly blocked (item 4's "trees older than 1000 years" still stuck at 3 site-wide, item 1 is Hidde's own in-session track), moved to the one piece of new-city work still open under CLAUDE.md rung 6's standing exception: Sintra, needed alongside Palermo and Cadiz to complete the "Top 10 tree city trips of Europe" collection. That collection can now finally be assembled and route-plotted, since all three of its missing cities are live.

- **Portugal's ICNF classified-tree register turned out to be directly queryable this session** (the live ArcGIS endpoint, not just the dados.gov.pt portal earlier sessions found blocked): queried it for Sintra concelho and got exact GPS coordinates, government decree numbers and measured ages for every legally protected tree in the municipality. Two of this city's five entries are sourced straight from it: the Plane Tree of Parque da Liberdade (id 274, classified 1951) and the Fern Cork Oak of Quinta do Relógio (id 311, classified 1996), both shipped `confirmed`. The register also independently confirmed CLAUDE.md's standing exclusion: id 275, the 800-year sweet chestnut at Quinta do Castanheiro, on private land and mutilated February 2025. Not researched further, not published, per the rule already recorded.
- **A real hard-rule-10 question resolved before publishing, not assumed away**: the Fern Cork Oak's register address ("Largo da Quinta do Relógio") sat right at the edge of a private estate, exactly the kind of ambiguity the rule exists for. Dug until an explicit source (Agroportal's coverage of ICNF's own fencing plan) confirmed the tree stands "no exterior da propriedade, junto da via publica", outside the property, at the roadside, which is also why it has a vandalism problem: tour guides walk visitors up to strip its bark as souvenirs, without anyone entering private grounds. Cleared to publish as a public roadside tree, with an honest access note asking visitors not to touch the bark.
- **A single-source entry shipped deliberately, not by accident**: the Pohutukawa of Monserrate has only one independent source (a garden blog) beyond monumentaltrees.com, which this project never cites directly. A second, larger pohutukawa at the same garden ("New Zealand One," bigger and more dramatic) was found and dropped rather than shipped as a sixth entry, because its only source anywhere is monumentaltrees.com and running with that alone would be exactly the fabrication risk hard rule 1 exists to prevent. Flagged honestly instead of padded.
- **Photos: 2 of 5 approved, both viewed directly this session** (Pena's walking cedar, showing the exact rooting-branch phenomenon beside an interpretive sign; Monserrate's Araucaria, towering over the treeline beside its lake, matching the park's own description). The other three (cork oak, plane tree, pohutukawa) are genuinely missing after a real search across Wikimedia Commons, iNaturalist and Flickr, not guessed at. Confirmed this session's tools can fetch an image via WebFetch and then view the saved file with Read, the same capability prior sessions found comes and goes.
- Shipped honestly at 5 trees, not padded to the usual cap. Four-tree minimum cleared with one to spare.
- Full sourcing, including every URL and the reasoning behind each flagged entry, is in `data/cities/sintra.json`'s own `notes` fields.

## 2026-07-28 — Cadiz: 35th city live, 5 trees, the second of three cities pre-approved to complete the Europe city-trips collection

Same session as Palermo above. A research agent verified and expanded on the three anchor candidates a 2026-07-27 session had already identified (Alameda Apodaca ficus, Parque Genoves dragon tree, Parque Genoves pohutukawa), and found two more genuinely verified candidates (an ombu and a floss silk tree, both also in Parque Genoves) plus a sixth, weaker one.

- **Shipped 5 trees, not 6.** The sixth candidate, a second Ficus macrophylla pair at Plaza de Mina, was dropped: it would have been a third fig-tree entry in one small city (the redundancy the research explicitly flagged), its age claim was internally inconsistent in its own municipal source ("two hundred years old" vs "planted early 20th century"), and its only second source was a stock-photo caption rather than real editorial confirmation. Five higher-confidence trees beat six with one weak link.
- **The throughline, used as the intro**: almost nothing on this list is native to Spain. Cadiz was an Atlantic trading port for centuries, and its two historic gardens (the seafront Alameda Apodaca, the inland Parque Genoves) were built specifically to show off exotic imports: a fig from India, a dragon tree from the Canary Islands, a pohutukawa from New Zealand, an ombu from Argentina, a floss silk tree from Brazil.
- **cad_002, the dragon tree, is now the last of its kind standing in Cadiz.** The city's other, older dragon tree (Drago del Tinte) collapsed in a storm in April 2013; confirmed dead and correctly excluded rather than confused with the living one.
- **A real superlative handled carefully**: cad_003's pohutukawa is reported by two independent sources (a Spanish garden blog and a national news outlet, okdiario) as the oldest documented specimen of its species in Europe. Presented as "reported" rather than stated as fact per hard rule 8, since neither source is a primary botanical register.
- **A real access check under hard rule 10**: a candidate 250-year-old dragon tree at the Palacio Episcopal courtyard was researched and explicitly excluded, its public access could not be confirmed and one source calls it "hidden away" inside a building with disputed legal access.
- **Photos: 1 of 5 (the Alameda ficus), viewed directly before approval.** The other four were genuinely hunted (Wikimedia Commons, iNaturalist) and came back either empty or CC-BY-NC only, not usable under the CC0/CC-BY/CC-BY-SA rule; recorded as missing rather than guessed at.
- **All 5 pins ship honestly `approximate`**: coordinates are park or promenade level (from OpenStreetMap place lookups), not tree-specific surveys. Unlike Palermo, Spain has no equivalent of MASAF's per-tree GIS register readily available this session, a real difference in source availability between the two countries worth naming for future depth passes.
- meta_description, question page fields and a 4-question FAQ block written fresh per Contract B/C.
- New city-list.json entry added (Cadiz was not on the original 100-city list; added at tier 2, status needs_curation).

## 2026-07-28 — Daytime extra-runs experiment: one run cut mid-work, lesson recorded

- Hidde freed his morning usage window for extra daytime runs. Outcome: one dispatched run worked 12 minutes (75 turns) and was cut by the usage limit BEFORE its first commit; in CI, uncommitted work evaporates with the runner. Cron retries died instantly. Net yield: zero.
- Lesson 1: daytime dispatches only pay when the interactive session is also quiet; the window is shared.
- Lesson 2, for every run: make the first commit as early as possible inside an item (smallest committable step first), so a mid-item cutoff loses minutes, not the item. A run that researches for ten minutes before its first commit is gambling all ten.

## 2026-07-28 — A new reusable technique: a hand-written binary XLS parser unlocks Italy's MASAF register, 3 real pin corrections (Rome, Bologna, Verona)

New session. Ran `python3 scripts/visitors.py` first: 66 visits, 100 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17), rising toward the end of the week. No reader submissions (CSV still header row only). Site healthy at session start, build clean, nothing published found wrong.

**State check before doing anything**: depth-exit-test at 5/8 (Rome, Tokyo, Paris, London, Barcelona pass; Amsterdam, Lisbon, Istanbul short), PRODUCT_TODO.md fully done or honestly blocked per today's earlier entries. Independently re-verified, rather than trusted blind, the three leads earlier sessions today flagged as "genuinely unexplored, not exhausted": `anitagaclar.gov.tr` (still connection-refused, a real reachability dead end, not transient), Commons' structured-data SPARQL endpoint (still redirects to an OAuth login wall), and the Andalucia singular-tree KML register (a later same-day entry already ruled it out as a structural land-type mismatch, not a parsing gap). All three confirmed genuinely dead for today's tooling; nothing new to add there.

**Found real, unworked ground instead: Italy's MASAF national monumental-trees register (masaf.gov.it), the source three separate sessions today already flagged as real but blocked ("this session's tooling can't parse it reliably").** It is a legacy Excel 97-2003 binary file (OLE2/BIFF8), and this environment has no `xlrd`, `openpyxl`, `pandas`, `libreoffice` or any other converter, and installing one is a new dependency, off-limits under hard rule 5. Rather than accept the same dead end a third time, wrote a small pure-stdlib OLE2 compound-file and BIFF8 record parser (reads the FAT/MiniFAT sector chains, the Shared String Table, and LABELSST/NUMBER/RK cell records) to extract the data deterministically instead of via lossy text extraction.

**Caught and fixed one real bug in the parser before trusting any output**: the workbook has two sheets ("Alberi Monumentali [Regione]" and "Eliminazioni", a deletions log), and a first pass merged both sheets' cell records into one grid, risking silent corruption where row/column coordinates collided between them. Restricted extraction to the records between the first two sheet BOF markers before using any value.

**Verified the parser against data already trusted on the site before applying anything new**: ver_003 (Ginkgo of Piazza Indipendenza, already `confirmed` from an earlier session's read of this exact file) came back at 45.4432, 10.9995 from a fresh parse, matching the stored point within meters. Also fetched Verona's file twice independently (a fresh, non-cached fetch) and confirmed byte-identical output both times, directly answering the "tool's reads were inconsistent across attempts" concern the ver_004 entry's own notes recorded as the reason it was never resolved.

**Three real corrections applied, all upgraded to `confirmed`, each cross-checked against details already in the story (species, address, or cited measurements) rather than taken on the registry's word alone:**
- **ver_004, The Himalayan Cedar of Via Nino Bixio, Verona**: the register's own GIS point for "Cedro dell'Himalaya" at "Via Nino Bixio 2" (matching this entry's species and street exactly), about 175m from the old street-wide estimate.
- **bol_003, The Plane Tree of Piazza Minghetti, Bologna**: the register's "Platano comune" at Piazza Minghetti lands about 11m from the OSM tree node a 2026-07-27 pass had already tightened this pin to but left `approximate` for lack of a second source naming the specific tree. Two independent sources now converge on the same point.
- **rom_001, The Ginkgo of Villa Sciarra, Rome**: the strongest match of the three. The register's "Ginco" at "Villa Sciarra - Viale Adolfo Leducq" carries circumference 310cm and height 25.0m, matching this story's own already-cited figures ("just over three metres around," "reaches 25 metres") exactly, not just a name match. About 220m from the old estimate.

**Cross-checked every other approximate pin in Italy's five most-populated regions in this register (Veneto, Emilia-Romagna, Lombardia, Campania, plus Lazio for Rome) against every remaining approximate entry in Venice, Milan and Naples; none of the rest matched.** Venice's ten entries are honestly ensemble/island trees per earlier sessions' own finding, and the handful of individually-named specimens the Veneto file does carry near Venice (a Canary palm on San Servolo, black poplars at Forte Gazzera, a hackberry in Santa Croce) fail on species or address when checked against our specific stored trees, so applying them would have meant guessing at a different individual tree than the one our entry describes. Milan's one remaining gap (mil_008, the Paulownia of Via Madonnina) and Naples' one remaining gap (nap_008, the Pine of Posillipo) are both genuinely absent from the register by species, matching what earlier sessions already found by other routes.

**No photos in this register**, it is coordinates and measurements only, so the site's photo count is unchanged at 191/328. Depth-exit-test unaffected (Rome already passed; this only tightened its precision further).

**Worth recording plainly for future sessions**: `masaf.gov.it`'s regional download URLs (pattern: `ServeAttachment.php/L/IT/D/<region-code>/P/BLOB%3AID%3D11260/E/xls?mode=download`) cover 20 of Italy's 21 regions and are `CC BY 4.0` licensed data. The parser script isn't committed to the repo (a one-off research tool, not product code), but the technique, fetch a region's xls via `WebFetch` on the raw URL to get a local binary path, then parse OLE2/BIFF8 by hand, works and should be tried before writing this source off as unparseable again. Toscana, Piemonte, Sicilia and the other 15 regions remain untried this pass; worth a future session's time if more Italian cities need pin work.

Site rebuilt (517 pages), all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Dead-tree suspect sweep is DONE: the remaining 25 cities read in full, all clear, zero further replacements

Same pass, right after clearing Tokyo, Naples, Rome and Valencia (see entry below). With momentum and no external tooling needed (this is a read-the-existing-story-and-judge task, not a search task), read every remaining suspect's story in full rather than stopping at the usual one-city pacing: Amsterdam, Antwerp, Athens, Barcelona, Berlin, Bologna, Edinburgh, Granada, Istanbul, Kyoto, Lisbon, London, Lyon, Malaga, Milan, Munich, New York, Nice, Paris, Porto, Prague, Venice, Verona, Vienna, roughly 40 entries.

**Result: every single one describes a living tree today.** The original suspect list was built from a keyword scan for death-adjacent language, which mostly caught false positives: wars, fires, felled neighbours, cemeteries, a dead king who once lived in the house, a burial site with a living tree over it, other non-collectible trees mentioned as context. Reading the actual story against the one real test (is the ENTRY's tree alive today?) clears essentially all of them immediately.

**Five genuine living-succession or living-in-decline cases worth naming, because they are the closest calls and all already handled honestly:**
- **ath_001, The Sacred Olive Tree of the Acropolis**: replanted from cuttings at least twice (WWII damage, then 1917 or 1952), the entry states this plainly rather than claiming continuous unbroken growth since antiquity. Passes, same pattern as par_007.
- **gra_002, The Sequoias of the Generalife's Upper Gardens**: the entry explicitly separates a different, older "mystery tree" that died before 2002 from the 1854-56 planting that is this entry's actual subject and stands today. Already correctly scoped.
- **ver_005, The Cypress Avenue of Giardino Giusti**: the single famous 600-year cypress Goethe wrote about was destroyed by an August 2020 storm, and the entry says so without softening it, describing today's avenue as replanted younger cypresses alongside older survivors the storm didn't reach. The entry's own subject is the avenue, not the one lost tree, and it is honest that visitors won't stand under the specific tree that made it famous.
- **ber_006, The Bellevue Oak**: the only entry that calls its own subject "not a thriving tree, it is a dying one," under two decades of arborist intervention. Still alive and standing today, which is what the rule actually requires; a declining, propped veteran is a different thing from a carcass, and the entry is already maximally honest about its condition rather than dressing it up.
- **ist_005, The Çengelköy Plane**: uprooted whole by a 2017 storm, propped back up by city crews, now visibly regrowing. A recovery story, not a death.
- None of these needed a rewrite; all five already tell the truth about what actually happened, which is exactly what the honesty rules ask for.

**The sweep Hidde asked for on 2026-07-27 is complete.** Every suspect the original keyword scan flagged has been individually read and judged. Total tally across all sessions: 2 confirmed dead and replaced before the sweep began (lon_005, vie_002, both already live with their replacements), roughly 60 suspects checked, 0 further replacements needed. The standing rule (judge new trees the same way, living-succession passes when told honestly) stays in CLAUDE.md/Step 1 for all future research; there is no longer a backlog to work through.
- No site rebuild needed (no tree data changed). Pushed alongside the Tokyo/Naples/Rome/Valencia clearance commit.

- **vlc_008, The Palm of Vicente Peris: corrected about 525m, upgraded to confirmed.** Among 158 catalogued Washingtonia palms citywide, exactly one carries a name, "La Palmera de Vicente Peris" (IPA 673), an exact match to this entry's own title, distinguishing it from every other anonymous palm record.
- **Three real dead ends found via the same trustworthy source, not tooling failure**: vlc_005 (Holm Oak, Avenida de Aragon) has zero catalogue presence anywhere near the city, the region's ~120 Quercus ilex records are all rural encinas far outside Valencia proper, this urban traffic-island tree was never eligible for the catalogue in the first place. vlc_006 (Los Tres Amigos) returned zero matches on any plausible search term, consistent with its own story: a 2015 neighbourhood-association adoption, not an official regional designation. vlc_010 (Kashmir Cypress) returned zero Cupressus cashmeriana records citywide, a genuine species absence. vlc_007 (La Mas Delgada del Paseo) has real headroom in the data (many unnamed Washingtonia robusta at Jardines del Real) but no way to single out "the tallest, thinnest one" among them without guessing by height alone, the same ambiguity this project has declined to guess through elsewhere; left approximate.
- Valencia now at 5/10 approximate pins (from 7), photos unchanged at 2/10.

## 2026-07-28 — A new source found (Italy's national MASAF monumental-trees registry), but this session's tooling can't parse it reliably; no data changed

Same session, after the single-source audit below. Went looking for an Italian equivalent of Turkey's queryable `turkiye_anit_agaclari` GIS layer, which has paid off repeatedly today, to help Verona, Milan, Naples, Bologna and Florence, all still below the photo floor with real approximate pins.

- **Found a real one**: MASAF (Italy's Ministry of Agriculture) publishes "l'Elenco degli alberi monumentali d'Italia" as per-region Excel downloads at `masaf.gov.it`, direct-downloadable (no login) for at least Veneto, Lombardia, Emilia Romagna, Toscana and Campania. Also found `ilregistrodeglialberi.it` (RAMI), a large volunteer-run database (nearly 18,000 trees, 11,000 photos) with coordinates, but its photos are explicitly all-rights-reserved ("Tutti i diritti riservati"), same category as monumentaltrees.com: usable for verification only, never for photos or as a citable source on its own.
- **The Excel files do not parse reliably with this session's tools.** A first pass via `WebFetch` on the raw `.xls` download appeared to extract real rows (coordinates for Verona's Piazza Indipendenza entries matched the site's own already-confirmed ver_002/ver_003 pins closely, a reassuring sign), but a second, more detailed pass asking for full row content on Naples' Capodimonte entries came back admitting the binary Excel data "resulted in illegible output" and could not be read reliably. Since the first pass's apparent success can't be distinguished from a lucky partial read without that reliability, nothing new was applied from it: not Naples' nap_001 pin (two ambiguous, ~600-900m-apart Capodimonte candidates with no species column confirmed), not anything else.
- **No data changed.** This is a genuine, real lead, not a dead end, blocked specifically by this session's spreadsheet-parsing tooling (this environment could not run a proper `xlsx` parser via Bash/Python this session, network access for that path being permission-gated) rather than by the source itself being unusable. Worth retrying with a session that can actually load and parse the file (openpyxl or equivalent) rather than relying on an AI model's lossy text conversion of binary spreadsheet data, which risks exactly the kind of unverifiable-precision the project's honesty rules forbid.
- Site rebuilt (no changes to publish, ran to confirm nothing was left broken), all contracts validated.

The "century-old" age had no source at all. What is actually documented, across two independent Italian-language accounts of a 2008 event plus a third general source, is real and better: three young Cedars of Lebanon planted 21 January 2008, brought from Bcharre and the Cedars of God forest by a Venice Academy of Fine Arts student, an artistic pilgrimage. Rewrote the entry to the true story and age (about 18 years, not centuries), kept the id, and added a "Recent planting, ancient provenance" label so nobody expects a century-old trunk, the same pattern already used for Lisbon's Giant Rubber Tree. This ranks as rung 3 (something published being wrong), which outranks ordinary depth work, so it was fixed ahead of continuing the photo/pin ladder.

**Granada gra_002, a real pin correction, ~750m.** A prior session's own entry (see "Granada, the worst-covered city" below) had flagged an unresolved open thread: 3 iNaturalist observations of Sequoia sempervirens clustering near "Patio de la Guardia", a different named spot than the entry's stored "Jardines Altos" location, with no way to tell if they were the same place. Checked: all three iNaturalist observations carry the field label "Secuoyas del Generalife", an exact name match to this entry, and converge tightly (within a few metres of each other); OpenStreetMap/Nominatim independently names a POI at the same spot "Generalife, Patio de la Guardia", about 130m from the iNaturalist cluster and roughly 750m from the old stored pin. Two independent sources converging within 130m of each other clears the bar for a correction. Upgraded to confirmed. The photos themselves are all-rights-reserved, so no photo gain.

## 2026-07-28 — New York's Hero Park yew gets a real pin upgrade via NYC's own Forestry Tree Points dataset; two other leads confirmed genuine dead ends

Visitors: 65 visits, 99 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17). No reader submissions (form still header row only). Site healthy at session start, nothing published found wrong. Depth-exit-test bar checked directly: Amsterdam (7/10 photos, 0 approx), Lisbon (6/10, 0 approx) and Istanbul (2/10, 5 approx) remain short; all three are documented exhausted across many passes earlier today (Wikidata SPARQL, Naturdenkmal-filename search, Openverse direct API, Mapillary/Commons-OAuth blocked by credentials this session doesn't have either), so re-running the same techniques on them again would be the looping CLAUDE.md warns against. Moved to the rest of the lead group and marquee Europe per rung 5's order; New York (4/10 photos, 5 approximate, Tier 1, published early) had real untried headroom.

- **New technique found for New York specifically: NYC Open Data's Forestry Tree Points dataset (`data.cityofnewyork.us`, resource `hn5i-inap`) is queryable directly as JSON/SoQL** (`genusspecies` field, e.g. `Taxus baccata - English yew`, plus `dbh` and a `geometry`/`location` point), and it had already resolved one New York pin (nyc_007) in a prior session without being named as a repeatable technique. Queried it properly this time against New York's four remaining non-cemetery approximate trees.
- **nyc_008, The Yew of Hero Park: pin corrected, about 1.4km, and upgraded to `confirmed`.** The dataset carries exactly one `Taxus baccata` point inside Hero Park's own OpenStreetMap boundary (confirmed via Nominatim: park centroid 40.6302790, -74.0878730, bounding box 40.6296234-40.6309550 / -74.0893022--74.0869446), an unusually large 48-inch DBH specimen, versus every other yew citywide in this dataset topping out in the teens and 20s (one outlier at 56" elsewhere, in Queens, not relevant here). A single, size-plausible, uniquely-located candidate inside a small three-acre park is a real match, not a guess. The old coordinate was a bare street-corner estimate for the Victory Boulevard/Louis Street intersection. Two Commons photos of the park entrance (`Hero Park Victory Blvd 2021 jeh.jpg`, CC BY 4.0, and `Hero Park on Victory Blvd HDR 2021 jeh.jpg`, CC BY-SA 3.0, both Jim.henderson) were found and viewed directly (fetching the raw file URL, reading the saved binary as an image) but declined as this tree's photo: both show the park's general tree line from the street, not this specific yew isolated from the other 143 memorial evergreens.
- **nyc_006, The Manchurian Linden of Kissena Park: genuine dead end.** `Tilia mandshurica` (and the alternate spelling `mandschurica`) doesn't appear anywhere in the Forestry Tree Points dataset at all, the same "species absent from the register" shape already logged for other cities' rarer cultivars. iNaturalist has no New York observations of the species either; the only global hits are in Switzerland and Russia, useless for identifying this specific tree even if licensed.
- **nyc_004, The Sassafras of Green-Wood: genuine dead end, and expected.** Green-Wood is a private cemetery, not a city park, so it falls outside NYC's own forestry dataset by definition (zero results for `Sassafras` in a bounding box around the cemetery). No further pin work possible without the cemetery's own survey data, which isn't public.
- **nyc_002, The Black Tupelo of the Ramble (Central Park): checked and stays approximate.** No `Nyssa sylvatica` point in the dataset falls anywhere near the Ramble's coordinates (nearest citywide matches are all in other boroughs or elsewhere in Manhattan). Genuine absence, not an unsearched gap.
- **nyc_009, The Red Oak of the Thain Family Forest (NYBG): a real near-miss, correctly not applied.** The dataset has four large red oaks (DBH 38-49") clustered right inside the Thain Family Forest's footprint, but four separately-located, all size-plausible candidates with no way to tell which one carries the Great Tree designation is exactly the ambiguity this project has declined to guess through before (Antwerp's ginkgo, Milan's bald cypress). `nycgovparks.org/facilities/great-trees/79` returned HTTP 403 this session, same wall other sessions have hit on that domain, so the disambiguating detail (if the page even has one) stayed out of reach. Left approximate.
- Photo hunt for New York's other gaps (Clove Lakes Colossus) found one real candidate, a Flickr photo by Matt Green explicitly titled "The Clove Lakes Colossus", but it's CC BY-NC-SA, unusable under the no-NC rule; the two iNaturalist observations already cited as a source for this tree are CC-BY-NC as well, consistent with why no photo has been used here before.
- New York now at 4/10 photos (unchanged), 4/10 approximate pins (from 5).
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Berlin, Vienna, Prague and Munich reach 10/10 photos: a new technique (searching Commons directly for photographers' own "Naturdenkmal [species] [park]" filenames) finds five real, viewed-and-confirmed photos after Amsterdam/Lisbon/Istanbul were re-confirmed exhausted

Visitors: 65 visits, 99 page views over 7 days (21-27 July: 8, 9, 10, 7, 4, 10, 17), still the strongest closing day. No reader submissions (CSV still header row only). Depth-exit-test bar re-checked directly: Amsterdam (7/10 photos, 0 approx), Lisbon (6/10, 0), Istanbul (2/10, 5 approx) remain the three short lead cities; Rome, Tokyo, Paris, London, Barcelona already clear it.

- **Redundant effort acknowledged rather than hidden**: this run's first pass re-hunted Amsterdam's 3, Lisbon's 4 and Istanbul's 8 photo gaps and 5 approximate pins (iNaturalist radius queries, Wikimedia Commons category browsing, Openverse, the Turkish national `turkiye_anit_agaclari` ArcGIS layer, its `image_url` field's licensing) without knowing beforehand that CURATION.md already documents dozens of prior sessions exhausting exactly these techniques on exactly these gaps today and on prior days. No new ground was broken there; every finding matched what earlier entries already recorded (iNaturalist hits exist but are CC-BY-NC/ND throughout; anitagac.istanbul and anitagaclar.gov.tr have photos with no stated open licence; Wikidata has no items for Istanbul's trees). Recording this plainly so a future run recognises the pattern faster: when CURATION.md already shows 10+ dead-end entries for the same city today, re-running the same search techniques is exactly the looping CLAUDE.md warns against, even when the run doesn't yet know that going in.
- **Pivoted to genuinely untouched ground**: checked reader submissions (none), the dead-tree sweep (complete, closed 2026-07-27), the `found_needs_check` queue (empty, all resolved earlier today), and PRODUCT_TODO.md (every item done, blocked-honestly, or Hidde's own in-session account-track work). Then checked which cities had NOT had a same-day depth pass despite carrying small photo gaps: Berlin (9/10), Vienna (9/10, 2 approx), Prague (9/10, 1 approx), Munich (8/10) had no 2026-07-28 entry anywhere in this file, unlike the marquee cities exhaustively covered today.
- **New technique, real yield**: rather than searching Commons by tree name or species+park keywords (already tried and exhausted on many cities), searched directly for the pattern these countries' own systematic Naturdenkmal photographers use as filenames: "Naturdenkmal [species] [park/street]". This surfaced real, previously-unfound files for all four cities' remaining gaps, each viewed directly (WebFetch on the raw file URL to a local path, then Read) rather than approved on caption alone:
  - **Prague, prg_010 (Neruda's Pear Tree)**: not a Naturdenkmal filename match but an iNaturalist observation (157617276, CC BY, Dave Richardson, 29 April 2023, place_guess "Petřín Gardens, Prague 1") about 200m from this entry's own triangulated pin. Viewed: a gnarled old fruit-tree trunk in white blossom, consistent with an old wild pear.
  - **Vienna, vie_002 (Ginkgo of the Schubert Monument)**: a prior 2026-07-28 session found only a photo of the tree's Naturdenkmal plaque and correctly left the entry `missing` rather than substitute a plaque for the tree. A same-photographer (GuentherZ) search this pass found the tree photo itself, taken minutes apart in the same 2010-02-27 sequence as the plaque shot (CC BY 3.0). Viewed both: the tree photo shows a mature bare-winter specimen above Stadtpark's facades, and the plaque photo reads "WIENER NATURDENKMAL NR: 569 GINKGO BILOBA" plus "FÄCHERBLÄTTERBAUM", the exact German nickname this entry's own story already quotes, about as strong an identity match as a citizen photo carries.
  - **Munich, muc_002 (Krüner Eiche)**: file "Stieleiche (Naturdenkmal) in Sendling-Westpark" (CC BY-SA 4.0, Tkarcher). Viewed: a massive, heavily gnarled oak with thick twisted limbs behind a garden fence, consistent with the story's storm damage and July 2022 protective fence.
  - **Munich, muc_004 (Beech of the Englischer Garten)**: file "Naturdenkmal Rotbuche Englischer Garten München" (CC BY-SA 4.0, Peterf), the photographer's own title naming this as the specific protected specimen. Viewed: a tall solitary beech in an open winter meadow, consistent with the park's open sections.
  - **Berlin, ber_003 (Steinlanke Oak)**: a Wiki Loves Earth 2024 upload titled "Naturdenkmal Stiel-Eiche Forst Grunewald Jagen 100" (CC0, Leonhard Lenz/GPSLeo), its own embedded GPS (52.451547, 13.191778) landing within about 45m of this entry's stored coordinate. Viewed: a large oak with a visibly damaged, hollow lower trunk beside a small building's roofline, consistent with the DLRG lifeguard station this entry's story describes.
  - One candidate declined on sight: a CC0 file "Eiche Grunewald LSG-38.jpg" looked promising by category match but viewed directly is an unidentifiable close-up leaf shot with no coordinates or landmark, indistinguishable from any of hundreds of oaks across the whole Grunewald forest; correctly not used.
- **Berlin, Vienna, Prague and Munich now all stand at 10/10 photos**, up from 9, 9, 9 and 8 respectively. Not part of the depth-exit-test's 8 lead cities, but real completions on marquee European pages nonetheless.
- **A second pass upgraded Vienna's vie_002 (Ginkgo of the Schubert Monument) pin to confirmed**: German Wikipedia's own Liste der Naturdenkmäler in Wien/Innere Stadt carries this exact tree's surveyed coordinate (48.206070, 16.380028, Grundstücksnummer 1343/1), read directly from the article's raw wikitext template rather than a rendered summary after a past session flagged this class of tool as unreliable on precise numbers elsewhere. That same table row's own photo field independently cites GuentherZ's file 0153 from the same 2010-02-27 shoot as the photo approved above, corroborating the photo match too. Vienna now at 1 approximate pin (vie_003, the Hauptallee chestnut avenue, which stays approximate on purpose: it's an ensemble entry with a deliberate midpoint pin, not a single-tree location gap). Checked Prague's own tree registry (prazskestromy.cz) for prg_010's equivalent; it gives only a descriptive location, no coordinates, consistent with what a prior session already found, so prg_010 stays approximate.

## 2026-07-28 — Product work: seasonality completion pass closes a real inconsistency (PRODUCT_TODO.md item 5)

Every below-floor lead-group and marquee-Europe city had already had a fresh research pass today (Dublin, Brussels, Antwerp, Malaga, Bologna and Verona above were the last untouched ones), so re-running identical photo/pin searches on already-exhausted cities would have been exactly the looping CLAUDE.md warns against. Moved to product work per the ladder's rung 7: PRODUCT_TODO.md item 5, the seasonality completion pass.

- A species-level audit across all 33 cities found the item's own literal done-when already satisfied (ginkgo, wisteria, wingnut, horse chestnut, magnolia: 0 missing `best_time`), but turned up a real, narrower inconsistency: four species with a documented seasonal peak already established on some entries (via existing `best_time` fields) were missing it on sibling entries of the identical species, an honest oversight rather than an intentional omission, since each of these species does have a real, specific moment (not a generic autumn-colour catch-all).
- **10 trees gained `best_time`, each written fresh from its own story rather than copied**: 3 Bald Cypress entries (Berlin's Charlottenburg swamp cypress, Lyon's Tête d'Or specimen, Madrid's Crystal Palace Pond grove) get their autumn needle-drop, the species' one genuinely conifer-breaking trait, already established on Milan and Seville's bald cypresses; 2 Black Locust entries (both of Paris's Robinier father-and-son trees) get their late-May bloom, already established on Antwerp and Granada's; 2 Black Mulberry entries (London's Charlton House and Evelyn mulberries) get their August fruiting, already established on Antwerp and Brussels'; 3 Japanese Pagoda Tree entries (Munich's Karolinenplatz specimen, whose own story text already says "flowers in August" without the structured field being set, plus Lyon's and Paris's) get their late-summer bloom, already established on Brussels'. Also closed while auditing: European Beech's autumn colour (Munich's Englischer Garten beech, Prague's Twin Beech of Hvezda, matching Edinburgh's already-set entry) and Northern Red Oak's autumn colour (New York's Thain Family Forest oak, matching Milan's two already-set entries).
- Left alone deliberately: Oriental Plane and Pedunculate Oak both have one entry each with `best_time` against 15-20 without. Unlike the four species above, ordinary planes and oaks don't carry a single well-known "worth the specific trip" moment the way a late-blooming pagoda tree or a needle-dropping conifer does, so the lopsided ratio reflects the site's own no-filler rule working correctly, not an oversight, and nothing was added.
- Verified: a fresh species-by-species audit script now shows 0 required genera missing `best_time` and 0 true evergreens (yew, holm oak, olive) carrying one they shouldn't.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

- **Overpass API queries for named or species-tagged tree nodes** near Farmleigh, Corkagh and Áras an Uachtaráin: hundreds of generic `natural=tree` nodes exist in all three areas (402 near Corkagh alone) but none carry a species or name tag that could identify the specific famous specimen among its unnamed neighbours; guessing which node is the right one would fabricate precision the source doesn't have, so none were used.

- **Openverse**: zero results for the Old Glebe yew or Corkagh's oak avenue; one Flickr hit for the Botanic Gardens yew walk, CC BY-NC, unusable.
