## 2026-07-28 — Porto's classified-trees register checked exhaustively for its 6 remaining approximate pins: all confirmed genuine dead ends, not unsearched gaps; photo hunt across all 6 gaps finds real coverage, none of it open-licensed

Continued down the marquee-Europe ladder after Seville: Porto (4/10 photos, 6/10 approximate) was next oldest-published below floor.

- **Queried Porto's own municipal open-data register of legally classified trees** (opendata.porto.digital, CSV and GeoJSON) by species and geographic radius for all 6 remaining approximate entries. Two, por_001 (Tulip Tree) and por_003 (Metrosidero), already used this exact source in a prior session; this pass extended it properly to the rest.
- **Result: all 6 stay approximate, and now for a documented reason rather than an unchecked one.** por_002 (Magnolia of Casa Tait) and por_004 (Plane of Quinta da Macieirinha): confirmed genuinely absent from the register within 300m of their site, consistent with their own notes that both are still "under formal review" and not yet legally classified, so they won't appear in a dataset of already-classified trees no matter how long this project searches it. por_005 (Camellias, ensemble), por_008 (Himalayan Cedar) and por_010 (Atlas Cedar): their species (Camellia japonica, Cedrus deodara, Cedrus atlantica) don't appear anywhere in this 575-row citywide dataset at all, a real absence rather than a missed query. por_007 (Texas Madrone) was never covered by this dataset either (same reasoning) and instead got a dedicated iNaturalist API pass: two real observations exist within 1km of the garden, one placed within about 60m of the stored pin, but neither is worth acting on: 60m is not a meaningful precision gain over an already-garden-level approximate pin, and it's a single casual-grade citizen observation, not a survey.
- **Photo hunt across all 6 gaps found real matches, all unusable**: iNaturalist has on-site observations for the Tulip Tree (exact coordinate match to the confirmed pin, a nice corroboration) and for the Alameda-style Platanus at Palácio de Cristal, but both are CC-BY-NC or unlicensed, excluded by the no-NC/no-unlicensed rule. The two Texas Madrone observations near the Botanical Garden are both fully unlicensed ("all rights reserved"). No Wikimedia Commons or Openverse coverage found for the Magnolia, the Plane, or either cedar specifically at these sites.
- Porto stays at 4/10 photos, 6/10 approximate. Full source list and exact coordinates checked are in this session's work so a future pass doesn't re-run the same queries expecting a different result once the "under review" trees (Magnolia, Plane) eventually get formally classified, at which point they should reappear in this same register.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Istanbul: a real new source found (anitagac.istanbul, İBB's own 517-tree database), but unusable for either photos or pin upgrades; two prior leads confirmed still blocked

Background pass, same session, re-trying the two untried leads flagged in this file's earlier 2026-07-28 Istanbul entry (Rome depth pass).

- **`anitagaclar.gov.tr`** (the "lar" domain, flagged untried): confirmed real (it's the national Ministry of Environment monument-tree registry, individual pages like `/detail/sakiz-pistacia-lentiscus/627` turn up in search) but still returns `ECONNREFUSED` on direct fetch from this environment. Not a dead end, a persistent access problem; worth a retry from a session with different network access.
- **The three İBB PDF route brochures** (Büyükada, Küçük Çamlıca, Gülhane): still unreachable. The Drive `/view` page only exposes viewer chrome, and the direct-download redirect exceeds WebFetch's 10MB cap. Filenames confirmed for whoever tries next: `BUYUKADA_AnıtAgacRota_TR_010224.pdf`, `GULHANEKORUSU_AnıtAgacRota_TR_010224.pdf`.
- **An unplanned real find**: `anitagac.istanbul`, İBB's own dedicated monument-tree database, separate from both domains above, 517 trees with individual detail pages (age, height, diameter, address, 6 photos each). It resolves three of Istanbul's five approximate-pin trees by street address, matching what's already sourced: ist_004 (Taşlı Çınar, Alemdar/Fatih, 446 years, matching this entry's own already-cited figure exactly), ist_009 (Ehrami Servi, Nizam/Büyükada, ~242 years, matching the ~244 already stored), and ist_007 (Ata Fıstık Çamı, Küçük Çamlıca, referenced but its specific detail page couldn't be located this pass, two guessed URLs turned out to be different pine trees entirely in Üsküdar).
- **Why nothing changed in the data**: this database gives street addresses, not coordinates, so it can't upgrade a pin already placed at that same address-level precision. Its photos carry no license statement anywhere on the site (only an İBB IT-department credit), so they're presumptively all-rights-reserved and unusable under the CC0/CC-BY/CC-BY-SA-only rule.
- Recorded rather than acted on: a direct email to anitagac.istanbul@ibb.gov.tr asking for exact coordinates and photo permission remains the single highest-value untried move for Istanbul's whole remaining gap, flagged here again since a prior session flagged the same idea and it's still nobody's job to send it (a run cannot write to third parties as Hidde or on the project's behalf without his sign-off; this would need him to send it, or to explicitly authorise a run to).
- No data changed. Istanbul stays at 2/10 photos, 5/10 approximate.

## 2026-07-28 — Seville (marquee Europe, oldest-first below floor): two pins upgraded to confirmed via the city's own tree inventory, four photo gaps stay genuine dead ends

Depth-ladder session. All three lead-group cities still below the depth-exit-test bar (Amsterdam 7/10 photos, Lisbon 6/10, Istanbul 2/10 photos and 5/10 approximate) have been re-confirmed exhausted across many prior sessions per CURATION.md's 2026-07-28 Rome entry; a background pass this session re-tried Istanbul's two specific untried leads (an alternate government domain, three İBB PDF brochures) rather than repeating dead-end searches, result pending. Moved to the next rung: marquee Europe, oldest-first among cities below floor. Seville (6/10 photos, 6/10 approximate) was the oldest-published marquee city still short.

- **Discovered Seville's own Parks and Gardens tree inventory** (ArcGIS FeatureServer, `services1.arcgis.com/hcmP7kr0Cx3AcTJk`, service `Parques_y_Jardines_Inventario_de_Arboles`, layer 13, "complete tree inventory updated in 2025"), the same source two prior trees' pins already used but not documented as a reusable technique. Queried it by species and geographic radius for every Seville tree still approximate.
- **sev_006 (Centennial Hackberries of María Luisa) upgraded to confirmed**: a dense cluster of Celtis australis specimens, several tagged mature phase, sits within about 30m of the stored pin in the inventory's own "Prado, Parque María Luisa" area, matching the entry's "clustered most densely near the Glorieta Rafael de León" description. Still an ensemble pin over a cluster, now government-inventory-backed.
- **sev_007 (Trees of the Alameda de Hércules) upgraded to confirmed**: dozens of Platanus hispanica individually tagged with the location "Alameda De Hercules, Plaza" itself, several in the oldest age phase, directly at the stored coordinate. An exact place-name match from the city's own inventory.
- **Two real dead ends via the same inventory**: sev_001 (Ginkgo Grove of the Real Alcázar) and implicitly the Patio de los Naranjos tree (sev_008) are not covered by this dataset at all, a genuine absence rather than an unsearched gap: both stand inside separately-managed heritage sites (the Royal Alcázar's own Patronato, the Cathedral chapter) outside the city parks department's own inventory scope. sev_001 stays approximate.
- **Camphor tree of the Glorieta de Doña Sol (sev_010): confirmed absent from this inventory too** (a species query for Cinnamomum/alcanforero returned zero results citywide), consistent with the entry's own note that this specimen rests on a single dedicated-blog source rather than an official register. Stays approximate.
- **Four photo gaps stay genuine dead ends this pass**: sev_003 (Figs of Plaza del Cristo de Burgos) has real editorial-stock coverage (Alamy, Getty, October 2023) and one blog photo with no stated license, none usable under the no-open-licence rule; a Commons file titled "Ficus plaza museo.jpg" was checked and ruled out, it documents a different Seville plaza's own singular fig (Plaza del Museo), not this one. sev_005 (El Gran Capitán) and sev_010 (camphor tree) turned up no Commons, iNaturalist or Flickr coverage naming either specific tree. sev_006 (hackberries, ensemble) likewise nothing tree-specific.
- Seville now at 6/10 photos (unchanged), 4/10 approximate pins (from 6).
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Edinburgh (UK focus region) and Venice: pin-tightening pass, no photo gains, all changes stay honestly approximate

Edinburgh sat at 10/10 approximate pins, the UK being the project's top-priority focus region per rung 4. Venice was in the same shape (10/10 approximate) with photo-hunting already exhausted in prior sessions, so this pass targeted pins only for Venice and pins-plus-photos for Edinburgh.

**Edinburgh: 2 of 10 pins tightened, 0 photos found.**
- **edi_007 (Camperdown Elm of St Cuthbert's)**: moved ~160-170m to the church's own OSM/Nominatim geocode, which suggests the earlier pin actually sat in Princes Street Gardens rather than at the church itself.
- **edi_010 (Corstorphine Sycamore)**: moved ~110-150m to the church's own OSM/Nominatim geocode, cross-checked against Historic Environment Scotland's listing for the same building.
- Both stay `approximate`: a building-address point, not a tree-specific one, since Overpass API was rate-limited/blocked throughout this session (worth retrying with a working Overpass connection, since UK sites are often well-mapped at the individual-tree level when the query actually runs).
- The other 8 trees (Craigmillar Castle Yews, Sweet Chestnut and Cedar of Lebanon at the Botanics, Cammo Ash, Monkey Puzzles of Lauriston Castle, Wentworth Elms of Holyroodhouse, Rockface Sycamore of Cramond, Tall Trees of the Hermitage of Braid) stayed genuine dead ends on pins. No photo cleared the bar for any of the 6 gaps: several near-miss candidates were correctly rejected on caption grounds (a Geograph Corstorphine sycamore photo turned out to be RBGE's separate conservation specimen, not the churchyard tree; a Lauriston Castle monkey puzzle iNaturalist observation sits ~560m away in a neighbourhood where the species is a common street tree independent of the castle). Two access limitations worth knowing for a future pass: `trove.scot` (Canmore's new host) blocks automated fetches with 403, and several council/heritage PDF site-plans (Cramond, Hermitage of Braid) failed with connection resets rather than confirmed absence.
- Edinburgh stays at 4/10 photos, now 8/10 approximate pins (from 10/10).

**Venice: 4 of 10 pins tightened (modest, all still approximate), 1 held back on identity risk.**
- ven_002 (San Michele cemetery cypresses), ven_005 (Certosa black poplar), ven_008 (Villa Groggia hackberries, the standout at ~400m from a district-level guess to the park's own mapped boundary), and ven_009 (Ca' Zenobio garden) all moved to better-sourced OSM polygon/relation centroids, each still ensemble- or property-level rather than a single specimen's surveyed point.
- **ven_006 (San Servolo olive) deliberately NOT moved**: a real geotagged iNaturalist olive observation exists 180m from the current pin, but the garden may hold more than one olive and nothing confirms this is specifically the named century-old specimen. Moving the pin toward an unconfirmed match risked making it less accurate, not more, so it was left as-is. Worth a future pass if a source ever names which olive is the old one.
- ven_001, ven_003, ven_004, ven_007, ven_010 confirmed as already at the best achievable precision (island/garden geometric centers, already matching or near their OSM polygon centroids).
- One content flag surfaced in passing, unrelated to pins: ven_003's currently-cited verification source (italianbotanicalheritage.com) doesn't actually mention a Cedar of Lebanon in its text, describing an Araucaria and a pomegranate instead. Worth a source re-check in a future pass.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Granada, the worst-covered city on the site (10/10 approximate pins, 1/10 photos): two photos found and approved, one pin tightened, a species discrepancy flagged

Granada had every one of its 10 trees on an approximate pin and only 1 photo, the worst combination on the whole site. Most of its trees sit inside or near the Alhambra/Generalife complex, which turned out to help: UNESCO-adjacent gardens are better mapped and better photographed than an ordinary city park.

- **gra_001, The Cypress of San Juan de la Cruz: approved.** Wikimedia Commons "Arbol_san_juan.jpg", CC BY 2.0, Jose Manuel ("Mazintosh"). Viewed directly: a large, heavily furrowed, gnarled trunk with a visibly broken, multi-stemmed crown, matching the story's own lightning-damage detail exactly. The file's own Spanish caption names this exact tree and garden ("Cedro de San Juan de la Cruz, en el Cármen de los mártires").
- **gra_005, The Plane Trees of the Fountain of Battles: approved.** Wikimedia Commons "Plaza_del_Campillo.jpg", CC BY-SA 3.0, Iblancasa. Viewed directly: one massive plane tree dominating the plaza, geotagged to the site, unambiguous. Granada now 3/10 photos.
- **gra_009, The Cedars of Carmen de la Victoria: pin tightened.** Moved from an estimated 37.1822/-3.5897 to 37.1809/-3.5890 (about 150-160m), anchored to two independent Nominatim geocodes of the property itself that converge within 15m of each other, corroborated by a separate address listing. Still property-level, not tree-level, so `location_precision` stays `approximate` honestly.
- **The other 7 stay approximate/missing, genuine dead ends this pass** except one open thread: gra_002 (Sequoias of the Generalife's Upper Gardens) has 3 iNaturalist Sequoia sempervirens observations clustering tightly about 880m from the current pin, at a different named viewpoint (Patio de la Guardia) than the story's Jardines Altos. Not applied: unclear whether this is the same tree (meaning the current pin is badly wrong) or an unrelated sequoia elsewhere on the grounds. Worth a follow-up rather than a guess either way.
- **One species discrepancy surfaced, not yet acted on**: Granada's own municipal tree register (granada.org's `arbolado` database) lists the Plaza de Gracia palms (gra_004) as Phoenix canariensis, not the Washingtonia robusta this entry currently states (sourced only to a single tourism site already flagged as thin). Worth a re-verification pass; the municipal register's own photos are non-commercial/restricted so not usable regardless of the species question.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Photo floor pass on the next oldest below-target cities: Madrid and New York each gain one photo, Kyoto's two near-miss candidates stay unresolved

Continued past the 8 lead cities into rung 4's photo-floor work, oldest-published-first among cities still below 8/10: Kyoto (7/10, needs 1), Madrid (4/10), New York (3/10).

- **Madrid, mad_003 (Three-Trunked Yew of the Velázquez Palace): approved.** iNaturalist observation 307498789 (Juan Cruzado Cortés, CC BY), coordinates 13-15m from the tree's own confirmed OSM node, species and cultivated-tag both matching. Viewed directly: dense yew foliage with multiple visible trunks, consistent with the entry's own three-trunked description. Madrid now 5/10. The other five gaps (Himalayan Cedar of the Crystal Palace, Holm Oak of the Trillo Roundabout, Aleppo Pine of Campo del Moro, Grandfather Cedar of Parque del Oeste, Stone Pine of the Priest's House) stay genuine dead ends: nearby iNaturalist observations exist for several but are CC-BY-NC (excluded) or too far/unconfirmed as the same specimen; Madrid's own Wiki Loves Monuments singular-tree catalog has the Campo del Moro pine registered under a different number (Nº118) with no Commons photo, only its sibling oak (Nº202) does.
- **New York, nyc_004 (Sassafras of Green-Wood): approved.** Wikimedia Commons file "Two Old Sassafras Trees, Green-Wood Cemetery" (Marty Aligata, CC BY-SA 3.0), captioned as the cemetery's own oldest trees. Viewed directly: two large old trunks a few feet apart with headstones in the background, an exact match to the story's own description of the tree appearing as a paired pair of trunks. New York now 4/10. NYC Parks' own "Great Trees" registry has official photos for several of the remaining gaps but its site terms state "All Rights Reserved" with no CC carve-out, confirmed directly rather than assumed, so it's not usable despite having the images. Kissena Park's Manchurian linden and Hero Park's yew stay genuine dead ends; the Clove Lakes Colossus has a well-matched Flickr photo but it's CC-BY-NC, excluded; three more (Ramble tupelo, Thain Forest oak, Van Cortlandt horse chestnut) have plausible-looking open-licensed candidates from the right park that could not be confirmed as the specific named tree without a direct look, held back rather than guessed, worth a future pass with fresh eyes.
- **Kyoto's two long-standing near-miss candidates (kyo_008 Taiko-zakura at Daigo-ji, kyo_010 Emperor's Gift Cherry at Kamigamo) stay unresolved and unapproved.** Real progress on one front: confirmed with independent corroborating sources that "Emperor's Gift Cherry" = Gosho-zakura specifically (not Saio-zakura, Miyuki-zakura or Kamo-zakura), worth keeping as settled naming even though it didn't unlock a photo. Neither candidate photo could be tied to its specific named tree by any caption, source or position match; both temples have multiple named cherries and nothing distinguishes the photographed one. Left `missing` rather than guess at identity. Kyoto stays 7/10.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Rome clears the depth-exit-test bar (8/10 photos); Amsterdam and Lisbon re-confirmed exhausted; Istanbul gets one more researched dead end plus a real unexhausted PDF lead

Depth pass in ladder order (Amsterdam, Lisbon, Istanbul, Rome per rung 5). Four parallel research passes, each explicitly told to avoid repeating techniques already logged as dead ends and to try genuinely new angles.

- **Rome, rom_003 (Twin Planes of the Eleven Fountains): approved, photo floor cleared.** Wikimedia Commons category `Category:Fontana degli Undici Zampilli` (the real category name; earlier passes had been guessing at plain-English names that don't exist on Commons) has `File:Scalone_Monumentale,_below_the_Fontana_degli_Undici_Zampilli_-_Orto_botanico_-_Rome,_Italy_-_DSC00275.jpg`, CC0 1.0, Daderot. Viewed directly (WebFetch on a `Special:FilePath?width=` thumbnail redirect, saved locally, read as an image): the monumental staircase flanked by two large trees with gnarled branches and mottled bark, an unambiguous match to the story's own description of the twin planes framing the fountain steps. **Rome now at 8/10 photos, 1 approximate pin: clears the depth-exit-test bar.** rom_004 (Cork Oak of the Orangery) and rom_009 (Aleppo Pine of Celimontana) stay genuine dead ends after this pass (Sapienza's own monumental-trees page has photos of both but no license statement anywhere; the national registry same; no Commons/iNaturalist/Flickr/Openverse hit clears the bar). rom_009 also reconfirms the known trap: the Wikidata-linked file that looks promising is still the mislinked Himalayan cedar, not the pine, correctly excluded again.
- **Amsterdam's three gaps (Heimanseik, Vondelpark Poplar, Amstelkade Olive Willow): re-confirmed exhausted.** New angles tried this pass (Amsterdam City Archive/Beeldbank, fresh Commons category browsing rather than keyword search, bomenbieb.nl, houtvanjestad.nl, Google Arts & Culture, Naturalis) all came up empty. `archief.amsterdam/beeldbank` is now structurally blocked by a bot wall (`beeldbank.amsterdam.nl` itself no longer resolves), the same failure shape as the already-known-blocked Commons SPARQL/Mapillary avenues. One near-miss correctly rejected: a 1949 Commons photo (CC BY-SA 2.0, IISG) of a tree at Amstelkade is a different, storm-felled tree at house number 42, not the surviving olive willow opposite number 154. That cross-street address (opposite Amstelkade 154) is worth keeping as location detail even though it yielded no photo. Amsterdam stays at 7/10.
- **Lisbon's four gaps (Coral Trees of Jardim da Luz, Nettle Tree of Avenida de Berlim, Ginkgo of Praça Paiva Couceiro, Giant Rubber Tree of Mouraria): re-confirmed exhausted.** The one flagged untried lead, Wiki Loves Monuments Portugal 2025, is a structural dead end for this purpose: it's scoped to classified *built* heritage (DGPC/IGESPAR), not the separate "árvores de interesse público" legal register these trees belong to, so it will never cover them regardless of how long the project runs. Closest near-miss: the Mouraria rubber tree has two iNaturalist observations taken at the exact spot, but both are CC-BY-NC, excluded by the no-NC rule. Lisbon stays at 6/10.
- **Istanbul**: one candidate found and rejected on sight rather than approved. ist_010 (Plane Trees of Beykoz Çayırı, an ensemble entry) got a CC BY-SA 4.0 Commons photo of the İshak Ağa fountain 65m from the stored pin, viewed directly: a mixed grove of ordinary-looking park trees around the fountain, none reading as an old, distinctively-barked plane. Too weak to publish on an ensemble entry that needs the trees themselves to be the recognisable subject; left `missing` rather than guessed. The other 7 photo gaps and 5 approximate pins stay dead ends this pass (Commons, iNaturalist, Openverse, Flickr, Turkish Wikipedia, İBB Overpass name-matching all tried again with no new result); `anitagaclar.gov.tr` (the untried "lar" domain flagged 2026-07-28 earlier today) still refuses connection, now confirmed twice, may be network/geo-blocking specific to this environment rather than the site being down. **One genuine new lead, not yet exhausted**: İBB's own borough-level "Anıt Ağaç Rota" (Monument Tree Route) PDF brochures on Google Drive cover Büyükada, Küçük Çamlıca and Gülhane, the exact groves containing ist_009, ist_007 and ist_004, with photos and route maps, but exceed this session's fetch tools' size/access limits (10MB WebFetch cap, no bash network access for a subagent). File IDs for a future session with a PDF-page-range tool or bash internet access: Büyükada `1vXWSIpr8nsLUPfYB2ighZFsuT60E7JQc`, Küçük Çamlıca `1XChc4C0BBjIAvQGObyvqyn0yfWp5HAka`, Gülhane `1YyToSLiU-EfRltRAzm3TA9nDW_oCbqd6`. Also worth a formal ask: `anitagac.istanbul` (a different subdomain from the licensed `data.ibb.gov.tr` open data catalog) has photos for most of these 8 trees but states no license anywhere; a direct email to anitagac.istanbul@ibb.gov.tr asking their terms could resolve most of Istanbul's photo gap at once if the answer is favourable. Istanbul stays at 2/10 photos, 5/10 approximate.
- **Depth-exit-test bar after this pass: 5 of 8 lead cities clear it** (Tokyo, Paris, London, Barcelona, Rome). Amsterdam (7/10 photos, needs 1 more), Lisbon (6/10, needs 2 more) and Istanbul (2/10 photos and 5 approximate pins, needs 6 more photos and 3 more pin fixes) remain short. Amsterdam and Lisbon's remaining gaps are now dead ends across a genuinely exhaustive range of techniques and several independent sessions; real further progress on either likely needs either new open uploads to appear over time, or credentialed access (Mapillary API token, Wikimedia OAuth) this environment doesn't have. Istanbul has the most real headroom left, via the PDF brochures above.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

- Extended today's Wikidata SPARQL technique to Naples, next in the ladder's order after the eight lead cities and the single worst-covered city on the whole site. Enumerated the full Naples monumental-tree Wikidata batch (14 items tagged "remarkable tree" located in Naples) so a future pass doesn't need to re-run that query.
- **nap_009, The Plane Tree of the Villa Comunale: a real, strong-on-paper candidate.** Wikidata item Q55741295 records this exact tree, its height (18m) and girth (428cm) matching the Campania registry figures already cited in this entry's own sources almost exactly, linked to a CC BY-SA 3.0 Commons photo ("Platano orientale.jpg", Sordelli). Held at `found_needs_check` rather than approved: the frame is a wide garden view with Washingtonia palms in the foreground, the plane's canopy visible but partially cut off, and this session's tooling still can't view images directly to judge the framing itself.

Checked against this entry's own data: nap_008 already carries the label "Young replacement," an age estimate of "planted 1995," and an age range of 30-31 years, meaning this project already researched and published it correctly as the 1995 replacement, not the dead original, consistent with the dead-tree sweep's own finding on this exact tree ("labelled 1995 replacement... explicitly described as such"). No fix needed, recording the check rather than leaving it silently re-discoverable.
- **Seven of the nine remaining gaps stay genuine dead ends**: nap_001 through nap_006 have no matching Wikidata item with a usable image within a reasonable radius (the nearest hits are a church and a fountain, wrong subject); nap_010 (Holm Oak of Sant'Andrea delle Dame) got a third candidate file checked and rejected on the same grounds as two prior candidates, frame dominated by the cloister's palms with no holm oak identifiable.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Istanbul's 8 photo gaps tried with the Wikidata SPARQL technique: genuine dead end across the board, one reason why worth recording

- Extended today's Wikidata SPARQL geo-image technique (which found real candidates for Rome and Barcelona, see below) to Istanbul's 8 remaining photo gaps specifically, since it hadn't been tried there before in dedicated form. Result: **none of these 8 trees, or their registry entries, have their own Wikidata item at all**, so the geo-radius query only ever surfaces unrelated nearby landmarks (mosques, fountains, museums, villas) rather than the tree itself. This is a structurally different situation from Rome and Barcelona, where the trees themselves are individually catalogued in Wikidata's monumental-tree register; Istanbul's own national registry (`turkiye_anit_agaclari`, used successfully for pins) apparently isn't mirrored into Wikidata the same way. Worth knowing before a future pass tries this same technique here again expecting Rome's result.
- One open thread, not a dead end: **ist_004 (Taşlı Çınar)** has two CC-BY iNaturalist plane-tree photos within a few hundred metres of its pin, near the Archaeology Museum/Gülhane area, viewed directly but not confirmably this specific tree. Worth a closer look by a future pass, ideally with a source that can place either photo exactly.
- Istanbul stays at 2/10 photos. No data changed.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — Amsterdam's three photo gaps tried again with the Wikidata SPARQL technique: genuine dead ends, but two real unexplored avenues found (not exhausted, just blocked by auth)

- Continued the same-day Wikidata-geo-query pass onto Amsterdam, the reference city, after it paid off for Rome and Barcelona. All three of Amsterdam's remaining gaps (Heimanseik, Vondelpark Poplar, Amstelkade Olive Willow) stay `missing`; this pass ruled out one specific false lead (a Ceescamel photo of a supported tree near Anjerperk, confirmed via the Dutch Wikipedia article's own wikitext to be a Robinia, not the Canadian poplar) that a less careful pass might have guessed at.
- **Two genuinely unexplored avenues, not dead ends, found this pass and worth a session with the right credentials**: (1) Commons' structured-data "depicts" (P180) SPARQL endpoint (commons-query.wikimedia.org) now requires Wikimedia OAuth login even for read-only queries, blocking a technique this pass wanted to try. (2) The City of Amsterdam has released roughly 800,000 of its own panoramic images as open data on Mapillary (CC-BY-SA), which would very plausibly cover a Vondelpark path tree and an Amstelkade street tree, but Mapillary's viewer is a JS app and its API needs a client access token, neither reachable by this session's unauthenticated fetch tools. Recording both so a future run with API credentials doesn't have to rediscover them, and so nobody mistakes "blocked by auth" for "searched and absent."
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-28 — A working image-viewing technique found: all three pending photo candidates viewed directly and resolved (2 approved, 1 approved with an honest framing caveat); Barcelona now clears the depth-exit-test photo floor

- The three `found_needs_check` candidates recorded earlier today (rom_001, bcn_001, nap_009) stayed unresolved because this session's tools couldn't render an image directly, the same gap flagged in the 2026-07-27 evening entry below. Found a working path: `WebFetch` on a raw `upload.wikimedia.org` file URL fails to describe the image as text (as expected) but saves the binary to a local path in the tool-results directory, and `Read` on that local path renders the actual image. For files too large for WebFetch's 10MB cap, fetching a Commons `Special:FilePath` URL with a `?width=` parameter redirects to a smaller `/thumb/` version that fits.
- **rom_001, The Ginkgo of Villa Sciarra: approved.** Viewed directly: an unmistakable ginkgo in full golden autumn foliage over a leaf-carpeted clearing, a visitor path and sign in frame, exactly matching this entry's own November best_time description. Rome now at 7/10 photos.
- **nap_009, The Plane Tree of the Villa Comunale: approved.** Viewed directly: a tall plane tree with the mottled, peeling bark characteristic of Platanus orientalis, apartment blocks and a statue behind it, consistent with the Riviera di Chiaia setting. A clean, confident match. Naples now at 2/10 photos.
- **bcn_001, The Holm Oak of Carrer de l'Encarnacio: approved, with an honest caveat kept in the record.** Viewed directly: the photo shows a wooden-fenced garden bed and a sign reading "Aquest corralet protegeix les arrels de l'alzina" ("This enclosure protects the oak's roots"), an explicit textual identification of this exact tree, with a dark evergreen canopy visible overhanging the top-right of the frame. Not a full portrait, the tree itself is only partially in frame, but the sign's own words are stronger identity confirmation than most park photos carry, so approved rather than held back on framing alone. **Barcelona now at 8/10 photos, 0 approximate: clears the depth-exit-test floor.**
- Worth recording plainly for the next run that hits the same wall: this technique works and should be tried before accepting "can't view images this session" as final.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

- **New technique**: delegated research explicitly using Wikidata SPARQL geo-radius queries for items with a P18 (image) property near each tree's coordinates, plus local-language Wikipedia articles and municipal open-data portals, rather than repeating the Wikimedia-category/iNaturalist/Flickr/Openverse searches already exhausted on these same gaps.
- **Rome's Ginkgo of Villa Sciarra (rom_001)**: a real find. Wikidata carries this tree in Rome's official monumental-tree register, tagged with the exact address ("Villa Sciarra - Viale Adolfo Leducq"), linked to a CC BY-SA 4.0 Commons photo uploaded via Wiki Loves Monuments Italia 2021 (Carlo8688), file description naming the species. Strong identity match on paper. This session's tools could not open/view the image file directly (no working outbound file-fetch this run, same limitation flagged in the 2026-07-27 entry below), so it's recorded as `found_needs_check` with the URL/licence/attribution in place rather than approved on text description alone. Rome's other three gaps (Twin Planes of the Eleven Fountains, Cork Oak of the Orangery, Aleppo Pine of Celimontana) stay genuine dead ends; the Wikidata item that looked promising for the Aleppo pine turned out, on checking the actual linked file, to depict a different tree (a Himalayan cedar) in the background of the frame, a Wikidata mislink correctly not used.
- **Barcelona's Holm Oak of Carrer de l'Encarnacio (bcn_001)**: a real find, weaker confidence. Commons category "Jardi de l'Alzina" (the informal name of the public garden built around this exact tree after the Salvem l'Alzina campaign) has a CC BY-SA 4.0 photo (Joe Mabel) showing the courtyard with the oak's canopy visible in one corner; a companion photo in the same series shows wall graffiti reading "SALVEM L'ALZINA", which confirms the site independently of the coordinates. Held at `found_needs_check`, both for the same image-viewing limitation and because the tree is only partially in frame, not a full portrait. bcn_004 (Himalayan Cedar of the Labyrinth) and bcn_008 (Silk Tree of the Ciutadella) both have photos on Barcelona's own municipal tree-catalogue pages, but the site's legal notice states all images are CC BY-NC-ND by default, failing both the NC and ND exclusions; stay genuine dead ends.
- **Lisbon's four remaining gaps** (Coral Trees of Jardim da Luz, Nettle Tree of Avenida de Berlim, Ginkgo of Praça Paiva Couceiro, Giant Rubber Tree of Mouraria) all stay dead ends after this pass; several near-candidates from the same locations turned out on inspection to be London planes or a war monument, not the named species. One lead worth a future look: Wikimedia Commons has an active "Wiki Loves Monuments Portugal 2025" project systematically photographing ICNF-registered trees under CC BY-SA 4.0, still growing (uploads through Nov 2025), not yet covering these four specifically.
- **Recorded honestly rather than left ambiguous**: this run's tooling could not fetch or render image files directly (the same gap the 2026-07-27 evening entry flagged), so neither `found_needs_check` photo above was upgraded to `approved`. - Picked Kyoto (6/10 photos) on the reasoning that famous named temple trees, unlike ordinary European park trees, are often heavily tourist-photographed. Paid off once: **the Five-Needle Pine of Hosen-in (kyo_003)** now has an approved photo, Commons file "宝泉院_01.JPG" (Category:Gohō-no-matsu, Hōsen-in, the tree's own Japanese name), CC BY-SA 3.0, Maechan0360. ## 2026-07-27 — Dead-tree rule sweep: both CONFIRMED entries replaced, Dublin's suspects verified (one real second violation found), one URL-redirect gap fixed retroactively

- Executed the sweep set up earlier today (see the entry below for the rule and the full suspect list). Both CONFIRMED-dead entries replaced with newly researched living trees, same ids reused:
  - **lon_005**: Queen Elizabeth's Oak (fallen 1991) &rarr; **The Sweet Chestnut of Greenwich Park**, one of the ~50 survivors of Charles II's 1660s Grand Plan avenue, individually recognised as a Great Tree of London per its own geotagged Commons photo caption. Age (~365y) is estimated from the avenue's documented planting date, not this specific tree, so flagged. Photo found (CC BY-SA 4.0, geolocated) but not visually confirmed this run (outbound file fetch was permission-gated with no user present), left at `found_needs_check`.
  - **vie_002**: Stock im Eisen (dead since ~1440, nailed trunk in a display case) &rarr; **The Ginkgo of the Schubert Monument**, Naturdenkmal Nr. 569, planted c.1900 in Stadtpark by city councilman Heinrich Braun. No photo found (only a Commons photo of its plaque, correctly not used to stand in for the tree). Vienna's city intro/question_context edited to remove references to the retired relic.
- **A real second violation found while verifying Dublin's four suspects (dub_001, dub_004, dub_006, dub_007)**: dub_001 (Hungry Tree), dub_004 (Addison's Walk yews) and dub_006 (Champion Planes) are all genuinely alive, pass. **dub_007, The Sculpted Cypress, does not**: its own story said plainly the 200-year-old Monterey cypress had died before UK chainsaw artist Tommy Craggs carved the dead trunk into a sculpture, the identical shape of violation as Stock im Eisen (a dead trunk kept as a cultural object). Replaced with **The Many-Trunked Holm Oak of St Anne's Park**, GPS-located via GBIF's Heritage Trees of Ireland dataset (CC BY 4.0, field-surveyed 2009, occurrence 368113488, recorded as an "arboricultural curiosity" with multi-stem form, 100-150 years old at survey time, projected to ~140 today), the same dataset already trusted elsewhere in this file. No photo found. Two candidates researched and rejected before this one: a cork oak and a possible Davidia involucrata at the National Botanic Gardens, both real specimens by secondary-source mentions but with no independently verifiable age or exact location, so left unused rather than published on a single thin source with an invented number. A Monterey cypress at Clontra House, Shankill (probably Ireland's largest, per monumentaltrees.com/Heritage Trees) was also considered, same species as the dead original, but the property is a private refugee accommodation centre with no public access, failing hard rule 10.
- **Retroactive fix: renamed trees never actually redirected their old URL.** Checking how the bcn_008 precedent (cited as the model for reusing an id) actually worked, found that `build_site.py` derives tree URLs from the tree's *name*, not its id, so a rename changes the public URL regardless of the id staying stable. Barcelona's bcn_008 swap on 2026-07-26 (Plane Trees of La Rambla &rarr; Silk Tree of the Ciutadella) never got a redirect: its old URL has 404ed for a full day. Added a small `RENAMED_TREE_SLUGS` list to `build_site.py` (old slug, new tree id, generates a redirect stub) and populated it for all four renames done to date (lon_005, vie_002, dub_007, and the retroactive bcn_008 fix). Verified all four redirect stubs resolve correctly in the built output.
- Every other city's suspects (ams, ant, ath, ber, bol, edi, gra, ist, kyo, lis, lon_003/lon_010, lyo, mlg, mil, muc, nap, nyc, nce, par, por, prg, rom, tok, vlc, ven, ver, vie_008) still need a pass; not reached this run. Continue one city per pass per the original instruction below.

## 2026-07-27 — Sweep continued: Naples clean, Rome's Quercia del Tasso was a third real violation

- **Naples (nap_004, 005, 007, 008): all four pass, no changes.** nap_004 (Yew of the Garden of the Princes) and nap_005 (Montezuma Cypress) are simply alive, flagged only by the suspect scan's keyword match on unrelated words ("sculpture", "Thule"). nap_007 (Platano of San Severino) and nap_008 (Pine of Posillipo) are the two cases this project already handled honestly before today's rule even existed: a regrowth-from-root and a labelled 1995 replacement respectively, both explicitly described as such in their own stories, both pass under the rule's own stated exception ("regrowth from old roots... and living replantings described as such... pass").
- **Rome (rom_001, 002, 007): a real third violation found.** rom_002 (Adonis) and rom_007 (Cedar of the Garibaldi Mausoleum) are alive, pass. **rom_001, the Quercia del Tasso, does not.** Its own story already hedged ("what remains of a monumental tree") without ever saying so plainly; checked directly against Italian coverage of a 2025 exhibition on the tree at the Museo di Roma in Trastevere, which states outright that the oak on the Gianicolo today is "secco, morto, corroso" (dry, dead, decayed), propped inside a small wall. Same violation shape as Queen Elizabeth's Oak and Stock im Eisen: a dead trunk kept as a monument rather than a living tree.
- Replaced with **The Ginkgo of Villa Sciarra**: planted in the early 1900s by American diplomat George Washington Wurts, one of Rome's roughly twenty official monumental trees (qualifying on age/size, landscape value, and botanical rarity all at once), in Villa Sciarra, a free public park his widow Henrietta Tower Wurts gave to the Italian state in the early 1930s. Sourced to Rome's own municipal monumental-tree page plus a dedicated Italian tree-registry page (verification only); the exact planting date rests on secondary sources rather than a primary record, so flagged. No open-licensed photo found (one exists on Flickr, all-rights-reserved).
- This also required removing rom_001 from **Europe's Ancient Oaks You Can Actually Visit** (a ginkgo doesn't belong in an oaks collection) and updating its counts (nine oaks/seven cities &rarr; eight oaks/six cities) and intro line referencing "a poet who rested in the shade", which had no other entry to attach to once Rome's was gone.
- Redirect for the old `rome/quercia-del-tasso` URL added to `build_site.py`'s `RENAMED_TREE_SLUGS` alongside the other three from the entry above; verified it resolves.
- Two cities now fully cleared: Naples (clean) and Rome (one violation, fixed). 25 of the original 64 suspects remain unchecked, spread across ams, ant, ath, ber, bol, edi, gra, ist, kyo, lis, lon_003/lon_010, lyo, mlg, mil, muc, nyc, nce, par, por, prg, tok, vlc, ven, ver, vie_008.

## 2026-07-27 — Top-10 tree city trips: research done in session with Hidde, draft ranking exists, three leads worth keeping

- Hidde green-lit the "top 10 vetste boom-citytrips" list in session ("run deze maar alvast"). Three research agents (Palermo+Cadiz, Funchal/Tenerife/Sintra, global sweep) plus a clustering analysis of all 33 live cities (trees within 2km of the densest point, greedy walk chains) produced a draft ranking, revised in the same session after Hidde set the deciding criterion: **the walk itself must be genuinely doable** (one continuous route, 45-90 min, honest about climbs, no transit mid-walk; note all chain distances so far are crow-flies, real streets add 20-30%). Hidde then scoped the first edition to EUROPE. Final walk-weighted Europe ranking: 1 Palermo (two short flat walks), 2 Seville, 3 Cadiz, 4 Lisbon+Sintra, 5 Porto (in BECAUSE of the walk criterion: 10 trees in ~1 hour), 6 Athens (trim to a ~60-min core), 7 Prague, 8 Rome (Gianicolo climb stated honestly), 9 Milan (flat), 10 Granada (only honest as a two-part walk). Seven of ten are already live; only Palermo, Cadiz and Sintra are new. Beijing, Mexico City and Buenos Aires are parked for a later world edition (research done and durable); Tokyo stays the separate November season special, dropped from the walking list because its trees are metro-spread. Publication bar set in session: an entry is only done when its route is actually plotted over streets with start/end and elevation, which is where the signature-walk work and this list merge. Honorable: Porto (best short walk of all: 10 trees in ~2km/~1 hour, but trees less epic), Savannah, Kolkata, Prague. Rejected as city trips: Tenerife (one-tree pilgrimage, Icod and La Orotava 25km apart), Funchal (garden city, almost no documented individual trees; Madeira sits outside the ICNF register).
- Key verified anchors for the not-yet-live cities are in the session transcript and reproducible: Palermo (Piazza Marina ficus 1863-64, free, "largest in Europe" only as attributed claim; Orto Botanico ficus 1845, Tree of the Year 4th 2023; Albero Falcone), Cadiz (Alameda Apodaca ficus 1903, Parque Genovés drago age disputed 130-200, pohutukawa "oldest documented in Europe" with May-June crimson bloom, ~2km flat seafront chain), Sintra (ICNF-registered 200yr plane in Parque da Liberdade free, 350yr cork oak, Monserrate metrosideros, Pena thuja), Beijing/Mexico City/Buenos Aires/Savannah per the sweep (ages as attributed ranges; Mexico City's named living ahuehuetes still need per-tree verification, flagged).
- **Publishing question: RESOLVED.** Hidde approved in session ("ja dat is akkoord", 2026-07-27): Palermo, Cadiz and Sintra may be researched as new city pages ahead of the exit test, exactly these three. The exception is recorded in CLAUDE.md rung 6. Runs can now: research the three cities to the full standard, plot each top-10 entry's walking route over real streets, then build the collection page.
- **Lead 1, big one for the 129 approximate pins**: Portugal's ICNF register is directly queryable (https://sigservices.icnf.pt/server/rest/services/BDG/AIP/MapServer/0/query, where-clause on concelho) and returns species, girth, height, age, decree and lat/lon for every classified tree in mainland Portugal. Check it against Lisbon and Porto pins.
- **Lead 2**: treefinder.parquesdesintra.pt, official Parques de Sintra GIS with a remarkable-specimens list for Monserrate; plain fetch returns nothing, needs browser-driven retrieval.
- **Lead 3 / correction risk**: the 800yr Sintra sweet chestnut in the ICNF register was mutilated Feb 2025 (declassification recommended) and stands on private land; must never be published.
- Nothing published or changed on the site; BACKLOG.md carries the direction and triggers.

## 2026-07-27 — Two new collection drafts, and a fix that also puts the two existing ones back in front of you

- **NEEDS YOUR APPROVAL: data/collections/ancient-oaks-of-europe.json**, "Europe's Ancient Oaks You Can Actually Visit". Nine oaks across seven cities (Berlin x2, London x2, Munich, Brussels, Rome, Madrid, Barcelona), pedunculate and holm, ordered roughly oldest-first: Dicke Marie (Berlin, 500-900y, disputed and stated as a range), the Krüner Eiche (Munich, ~600y), the Royal Oak (London, Richmond Park), the Fulham Palace Oak (London, likely Britain's oldest holm oak), the Humboldt Oak (Berlin), the Chêne Joséphine (Brussels), the Quercia del Tasso (Rome, legendary age explicitly flagged as unconfirmed), the Holm Oak of the Trillo Roundabout (Madrid) and the Holm Oak of Carrer de l'Encarnació (Barcelona, saved from a housing development by its neighbours). Every fact and every note is drawn from this project's own already-verified tree stories; nothing new researched or claimed. This is the first slate item from the "Open with Hidde" item 3 promise, oaks first.
- **NEEDS YOUR APPROVAL: data/collections/ginkgos-worth-a-november-trip.json**, "The Ginkgos Worth a November Trip". Ten ginkgos across nine cities (Tokyo x2, Vienna, Berlin, Prague, Milan, Bologna, Florence, Granada, Verona), every one already carrying a real, sourced `best_time` peaking in autumn (mostly November, two late-October starts). Anchored by Tokyo's 750 year old Zenpukuji Ginkgo, a firebombing survivor, and the Meiji Jingu Gaien avenue. Same rule: grounded entirely in already-verified data, no new research.
- **A real bug, found while checking how the second draft would actually behave on the live site**: both previously shipped collections (Europe's 10 Most Remarkable Ancient Trees; Trees Older Than 400 Years You Can Actually Visit) are still `status: needs_curation` in their JSON, exactly as logged when they were drafted ("NEEDS YOUR APPROVAL... ships with an awaiting-curation banner until you sign off"), but `scripts/build_site.py` never actually withheld them: they were linked from the homepage, from every city page's "More like this," from every question page, and listed with full schema in the sitemap. They have been fully public and indexable since they shipped, without you ever approving either.
- Fixed the build script so `needs_curation` collections build a real page (stable URL, so you or a future run can read the draft) but are noindexed, excluded from the sitemap, and not linked from anywhere else, the same treatment already used for the account-page prototype. Only a collection with a status other than `needs_curation` gets surfaced publicly. Verified directly on the built output: `/collections` currently shows an honest "none public yet" note, the homepage has zero collection links, `sitemap.xml` has zero `/collections/*` entries, and all four draft pages carry `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">`. Full detail and the exact diff reasoning in LOG.md.
- **What this means for you**: nothing published changed in content, only in whether it's currently linked and crawlable. All four collections are one status-field edit away from going live (or staying gated) exactly as you decide. Reviewing all four at once is probably easiest since two have effectively been live for a while already; approving is a status flip on the JSON's `status` field, same as approving a city.

Moved about 35m and set `confirmed`.

Giosue Carducci's tomb has its own Wikidata entry with a "most trustworthy"-ranked coordinate, cross-referenced to an OSM node named "Tomba di Giosue Carducci." This entry's own already-cited sources state the cypress stands directly over the tomb, so the tomb's precise position is a fair anchor for the tree even though the cypress itself isn't independently geotagged. Moved and set `confirmed`.

- **The Monumental Planes of the Montagnola (bol_001)** and **the Camphor of San Michele in Bosco (bol_010)** stay genuinely unresolved: the Montagnola has roughly 200 identically-tagged plane nodes with no distinguishing feature, an honest ensemble limit; no camphor-tagged OSM node exists anywhere near San Michele in Bosco at all.

## 2026-07-27 — Malaga: 5 of 10 approximate pins resolved, its pin dimension never touched since original research

- After New York, checked which below-floor cities hadn't had a same-day depth pass at all yet, since virtually every lead-group and marquee-European city had already been worked at least once today. Malaga (3/10 photos, 10/10 approximate) stood out: its photo gap had a genuine dead-end pass today, but its pins had never been worked since the city's original research, and three trees (mlg_001, mlg_006, mlg_007) sat on an identical shared coordinate, the La Concepcion garden's centroid, an obvious tell that at least two of the three were wrong. Two more (mlg_008, mlg_009) shared Parque de Malaga's centroid the same way.
- **mlg_006, the Hackberry of the Cypress Cottage**: an OpenStreetMap tree node tagged the correct species (Celtis australis) sits about 8m from an independent geocode of "Casita de los Cipreses," the exact building this tree's own name and the garden's own page place it beside. Two independent confirmations landing within a few metres of each other. Moved about 370m and set `confirmed`.
- **mlg_009, the Rare Cassia of Malaga Park**: an OpenStreetMap node tagged the correct, genuinely uncommon species (Peltophorum dubium) sits inside the park, moved about 160m off the shared centroid and set `confirmed`.
- **mlg_003, El Barrilito**: the existing pin itself, not moved, reverse-geocodes to "Fuente del Jardin del Palo Borracho," a fountain named after this tree's own popular Spanish name. Landmark match strong enough to set `confirmed` without changing coordinates.
- **mlg_005, the Fig of the Picasso Gardens**: an OpenStreetMap node for the Ortiz Berrocal sculpture "Siextasis" (installed 30 October 1977, an exact date match to independent press coverage describing it as standing between this tree and its twin) gave a tighter point, moved about 55m and set `confirmed`. This pins the adjacent sculpture rather than the trunk itself, but two sources independently place the sculpture between the two named fig trees, which is a fair basis for a garden-scale confirmation.
- **mlg_010, the Avocado of Ciudad de Jaen School**: the school's own OpenStreetMap building footprint lands within 6m of the existing address-derived pin. Set `confirmed`, coordinates effectively unchanged.
- **mlg_007, the Tallest Araucaria in the Province**: no tree-tagged node exists for this species in the garden. The garden's own page places it "beside the waterfall," and La Concepcion has two on-site waterfalls about 50m apart; pinned to their midpoint and left `approximate` rather than guessing between them, but this is now 370m+ from the other two La Concepcion entries it previously shared an identical coordinate with.
- **mlg_001 (the Transplanted Olive) and mlg_008 (the Native Oak of Malaga Park) stay genuinely unresolved**: no OSM node, registry entry or map source placed either more precisely than the garden/park they already cite. Andalucia's regional singular-tree catalogue was checked and ruled out for mlg_001: it explicitly excludes cultivated garden specimens, covering only non-urban land. Left as `approximate` rather than inventing a distinguishing point; mlg_008 still shares its exact coordinate with mlg_009's old position, an honest gap rather than a forced fix.
- **mlg_004, the Floss Silk Tree of Cortina del Muelle**: checked but not moved; the street itself is short enough that the existing address-level pin already sits well within its real extent, and no closer species-tagged node exists nearby (the closest Ceiba node found is a different species roughly 300m away, on a different street, correctly not used).
- Malaga now at 5/10 approximate pins (from 10). Photos stay at 3/10, already exhausted today.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Munich: all 9 remaining approximate pins resolved via its own Naturdenkmal register, cross-checked independently before trusting it

- After New York, checked Munich's overall stats directly rather than trusting an old log line claiming "already meeting the pin bar": it was actually at 9/10 approximate, the worst pin precision of any city with strong photo coverage (8/10). All nine gaps are individually named, legally protected Naturdenkmal trees, not ensembles, so unlike Venice or Nice this was a real search gap, not a structural honesty limit.
- German Wikipedia's "Liste der Naturdenkmäler in München" (already cited as a source on every one of these entries) turned out to carry a per-tree coordinates column, DMS format. First reaction was suspicion rather than trust: two different trees (the twin elms and the fern-leaved beech, both "near the Chinese Tower") returned almost identical coordinates, which looked like a possible copy-paste artifact in the table rather than real individual surveys.
- **Cross-checked before using it, on two independent axes**: (1) geocoded "Krüner Platz" directly via Nominatim for the Krüner Eiche; it landed within about 100m of the Wikipedia table's figure, and confirmed the *previous* stored pin (1.4km away) was the actual error, not the table. (2) The approved photo for the twin elms has its own Commons file description placing them "at the path fork about 200m northwest of the Monopteros"; geocoding the Monopteros and projecting 200m northwest landed within about 70m of the same Wikipedia table's coordinate for that entry, an independent confirmation from a completely different source. Two-for-two convinced this table is reliable at the individual-tree level, and the "identical" coordinates for the elms and the beech were just two different trees genuinely 100-150m apart in the same park corner, not a data error.
- Moved and confirmed all nine: the Krüner Eiche (1.4km), the Reichs- und Friedenseiche (1.3km), the Beech of the Englischer Garten (1.6km), the Twin Elms (1.5km), the Fern-leaved Beech (1.4km), the Oak of the Dörfchen (550m), the Hackberries of Oskar-von-Miller-Ring (415m), the Japanese Pagoda Tree of Karolinenplatz (280m), and the Wych Elm of Maximiliansplatz (85m, the only one that was already close).
- Munich now at 0/10 approximate pins (from 9), the first Naturdenkmal-heavy city to reach zero. Photos unchanged at 8/10, already clearing the floor.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Rome: Adonis gets a photo, a new image-viewing technique closes an old open question

- Continued down rung 5's order after Istanbul, to Rome (6/10 photos, 0/10 approximate, already at the precision half of the depth-exit-test bar). All 4 remaining photo gaps (Adonis, the Twin Planes of the Eleven Fountains, the Cork Oak of the Orangery, the Aleppo Pine of Celimontana) had been tried hard and left open across several prior passes, one specifically because "this session's tools could not open the image to check whether they're actually in frame."
- **New technique that unblocks that class of gap**: WebFetch can fetch a raw image URL directly; when it cannot describe the binary, it still saves the fetched image to a local path in the tool result, which the Read tool can then open and actually view. This works around sessions where a direct image fetch or curl download requires an approval that never comes in an unattended run.
- Used it to finally resolve **the Twin Planes of the Eleven Fountains**' one open candidate (a CC0 Commons photo of the fountain itself): viewed directly, it is a close-up of a stone garden urn against a hedge, no plane trees in frame. Correctly rejected, a genuine dead end now closed rather than left open.
- Used it again on **Adonis**: a 2009 CC BY-SA 3.0 Commons photo (categorized "Valle dei platani") viewed directly shows a large oriental plane with a prominent hollow trunk cavity standing in a grove of similarly aged planes, an unusually strong match to Adonis's own described hollow trunk and setting. The file doesn't name Adonis specifically among the grove's nine survivors, so approved as an honest illustration of the tree and its grove, the same standard already used elsewhere on this project (Amsterdam's Hortus cycad pair, Istanbul's Karacaahmet cypresses) for ensemble scenes.
- **The Cork Oak of the Orangery and the Aleppo Pine of Celimontana stay genuine dead ends**: Commons category browsing and direct iNaturalist API queries at both trees' exact registry coordinates (filtered server-side by open licence) returned zero CC0/CC-BY/CC-BY-SA candidates for either, only cc-by-nc and all-rights-reserved observations for the cork oak, nothing at all for the pine.
- Rome now at 7/10 photos (from 6), still 0/10 approximate.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Istanbul: two Bahçeköy pins tightened to specific age-matched trees, Beykoz Çayırı upgraded to confirmed

- Continued straight on from the pass below, querying the same national GIS layer (`turkiye_anit_agaclari`) with actual geometry returned and converted from Web Mercator to lat/lon, rather than relying on general campus/farm/park anchors.
- **The Oldest Plane of Bahçeköy (ist_001)**: found an individual record in the same forest aged exactly 1,382 years, an almost exact match to this entry's own 1,300-1,400 year range, about 1.9km from the previous campus-building geocode and genuinely inside the forest rather than among the campus buildings, which fits the story better. One real, unresolved discrepancy: the registry labels that record's species Platanus acerifolia (a hybrid too young a lineage to reach this age), while this entry's Turkish sources describe an Oriental Plane. Treated as a likely registry mislabeling rather than a reason to distrust the tree, given the extreme age match, but flagged honestly and kept `approximate` rather than upgraded.
- **The Ahtapot Çınar (ist_002)**: same technique, same result. A record 380m from the ist_001 point above, aged exactly 579 years against this entry's own "up to 580 years", moved about 500m from the prior farm-geocode. Same species-label discrepancy as ist_001, same call: kept `approximate`.
- **The Plane Trees of Beykoz Çayırı (ist_010)**: this one had no discrepancy to flag. Three individually registered Platanus orientalis, all within 150m of each other, ages 390/406/406, an exact match on species, count ("three or four individually protected") and age range. Pin moved about 260m to their centroid and upgraded to `confirmed`.
- Checked photo licensing on the same GIS layer's `image_url` field properly this time rather than just noting dead links: the live ones resolve to anitagaclar.gov.tr, an official Turkish government domain. Searched for its terms of use; general Turkish state-site policy limits reuse to personal/informational use and forbids alteration, not an open licence, so these photos are correctly off limits under hard rule 4, same treatment as monumentaltrees.com. Also tried Wikimedia, iNaturalist (direct API radius queries) and general web search fresh for Istanbul's 8 remaining photo gaps: no CC0/CC-BY/CC-BY-SA candidate found for any of them this pass, all genuine dead ends for now.
- Istanbul now at 6/10 approximate pins (from 7) and one of those newly `confirmed`. Photos stay at 2/10.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Istanbul: a national monumental-tree GIS layer found, 4 pins meaningfully tightened

- Istanbul has been the furthest-behind of the eight lead cities all day (2/10 photos, 8/10 approximate), with many prior passes hitting anitagac.istanbul's JS-only map as a wall. Found the real source behind it this pass: the underlying registry is published as a public ArcGIS Online feature service, `turkiye_anit_agaclari` (item 4d6560f37c554e059065f77a86b7e489, owner adem_cbskampus), queryable directly at `services5.arcgis.com/26ObnytmBZ58Wbj8/arcgis/rest/services/turkiye_anit_agaclari/FeatureServer/0/query`, covering all of Turkey with per-tree species, age, circumference, height and coordinates. This is the same kind of national open-data layer that has paid off repeatedly today for Rome, Madrid, Bologna, Brussels and Antwerp; Istanbul just needed the right endpoint.
- **The Oldest Plane of Bahçeköy (ist_001)**: the stored pin was a general campus-area guess, about 1.3km from the real forestry faculty. Geocoded İstanbul Üniversitesi-Cerrahpaşa Orman Fakültesi directly (Nominatim) and cross-checked against the GIS layer, which shows a dense cluster of exotic arboretum species (giant sequoia, American red oak, incense cedar) at essentially the same point, consistent with a forestry-faculty planting rather than ordinary forest. No individual 1,300+ year specimen is in the layer itself (plausibly too old/hollow to have been included in a standard survey), so this stays approximate, now anchored to the right campus rather than a guess.
- **The Ahtapot Çınar (ist_002)**: geocoded Bilezikçi Çiftliği, the farm this entry's own address already names, directly. Moved about 1.2km to the real farm location.
- **The Plane Trees of Beykoz Çayırı (ist_010)**: OpenStreetMap carries a leisure=park feature individually named "Beykoz Çayırı" in Ortaçeşme Mahallesi, and the GIS layer independently shows a dense cluster of a dozen-plus protected Platanus orientalis at essentially the same coordinates, two independent sources agreeing. Moved about 1km to the real meadow.
- **The Ata Fıstık Çamı (ist_007)**: the GIS layer has a Pinus pinea in Üsküdar aged exactly 397 years, matching this entry's own age figure precisely, though its circumference (520cm) doesn't cleanly match the already-sourced 145cm-across figure (roughly 455cm circumference), so tightened about 170m but kept approximate rather than forced to confirmed on an imperfect measurement match.
- **Checked and correctly not applied**: the Florya Mastic Tree (ist_008) has a cluster of Pistacia atlantica in Bakırköy, a related but different species from this entry's sourced Pistacia lentiscus, and no circumference in the cluster (650cm) cleanly matches the sourced "roughly 2 metres across" (~628cm) closely enough to be confident; left unchanged rather than force a species mismatch. Adalar (Büyükada) has zero records in this national layer, confirming the Ehrami Servi's (ist_009) genuine jurisdictional gap rather than a missed search.
- **Photo lead checked and closed, not left open**: the GIS layer's `image_url` field looked promising (direct photo links per tree), but avrupaanitagaclar.com, the domain behind roughly a third of the European-side entries' image URLs, has expired and now redirects to an unrelated gambling site; those links are dead, not merely license-unclear, and should not be treated as a photo source by a future pass. anadoluanitagaclar.com (the Anatolian-side domain) returned a bot-block (403) rather than a redirect, so its status is unconfirmed either way, worth a retry with different tooling but not assumed safe.
- **A fifth fix, found on a follow-up pass**: the Cypresses of Karacaahmet (ist_006), an honest nine-cypress ensemble entry, turned out to have real independent confirmation available. The GIS layer shows exactly 8 individually protected Cupressus sempervirens clustered within about 300m of the existing pin, matching the entry's own "nine cypresses" claim closely enough to treat the cemetery position as genuinely confirmed rather than approximate; refined the pin about 50m to the cluster's centroid. Also tried the same layer on the Taşlı Çınar (ist_004, 328cm circumference, 26m height): the nearest Fatih plane by coordinates has a suspiciously exact longitude match but a circumference roughly double (628cm), too weak to use without a supporting field, left unchanged rather than force it.
- Istanbul now at 7/10 approximate (from 8) and 2/10 photos (unchanged; pins were the win today).
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

No change to edi_001, edi_002 or edi_004 (the two Botanic Garden entries).
- **Dublin's Tree Register of Ireland** (treeregister.ie), a genuine national tree-level GPS registry, turned out to return empty page content on every fetch tried, consistent with the Tree Council's own admission that the register has been "on hold" for lack of funding; treat as unreachable for now rather than retry blind. Found and downloaded Dublin City Council's own open tree inventory instead (data.smartdublin.ie, CC BY 4.0, address-level not GPS-level) and located a dense run of Holm Oak and Pine street trees on "All Saints Park" road, tantalisingly close to dub_008's Holm Oak Avenue story (both near St Anne's Park, both about Lord Ardilaun's estate-boundary plantings) but roughly 650m from the current pin and not confirmably the same named avenue rather than an unrelated modern street-planting scheme. Left dub_008 unchanged; a future pass with a way to confirm the connection (an estate-boundary map, a park authority source naming the avenue's actual path) could close this.
- **One real fix made**: Dublin's Sculpted Cypress (dub_007) had a genuine, previously-unnoticed inconsistency. Its own photo-verification note (recorded by an earlier pass) already quoted the Commons caption correctly as "the junction of Watermill Road and James Larkin Road," but the entry's own story text and address still said "Clontarf Road," a road that does not meet Watermill Road anywhere nearby, and the pin was never updated to match. Verified independently via OpenStreetMap: Watermill Road's own mapped geometry ends at 53.3741,-6.1646, within 35m of the separately-mapped Bull Island Causeway entrance and matching the same photo caption's "opposite Bull Island Causeway" detail. Fixed the story and address text (now "James Larkin Road"), and moved the pin about 700m to this corroborated junction. Kept at `location_precision: approximate` since this locates the road junction the park entrance sits near, not a surveyed trunk position.
- **A second pin improved the same way**: Dublin's Provost's Plane (dub_005) was pinned to a loose Trinity College campus estimate. TCD's own published source states the tree stands in "the Provost's Courtyard at No. 1 Grafton Street," and OpenStreetMap has the Provost's House individually named at exactly that address (53.3436, -6.2587). Moved the pin about 260m to the building itself. Kept `approximate`, since the tree stands in the walled courtyard behind the building rather than at its address point.
- Two other Dublin candidates checked for the same kind of fix and correctly left alone: Corkagh Park's Oak Avenue (dub_010) has no matching named feature in OpenStreetMap ("Oak Downs" nearby is an unrelated residential street), and Farmleigh's and Áras an Uachtaráin's grounds (dub_009, dub_002) are large state properties without individually mapped internal features to anchor a tighter pin.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Valencia: 3 pins corrected via the region's own monumental-tree catalogue, one near-miss caught and rejected

- Continued down rung 5's oldest-first order after Nice, to Valencia (2/10 photos, 10/10 approximate, untouched since original research).   - **The Olive Tree of La Rambleta (vlc_002)**: the only Olea europaea recorded anywhere in the entire city dataset, marked at 350-plus years (matching this entry's own low end), sitting in a cluster of catalogued date palms and eucalyptus in the same San Marcelino/Patraix pocket the park occupies. Moved about 1.1km, the largest single correction found this pass.
  - **The Eucalyptus of Benimamet (vlc_009)**: named directly in the catalogue as "Eucalipto de Benimámet" (IPA 2375), with a 41.2m height matching this entry's own figure exactly. Moved about 790m.

Left the Ripalda ficus's pin unchanged rather than misapply a real dataset match to the wrong tree.

- Continued rung 5's marquee-Europe order (Berlin, already 9/10 photos and 0 approximate, needed nothing further) to Seville, at 9/10 approximate since research. sevilla.org's own singular-trees catalogue is still connection-refused (confirmed again this pass), but found a genuinely new source: Seville's Parks and Gardens department publishes its full 2025 tree inventory on ArcGIS Hub (`services1.arcgis.com/hcmP7kr0Cx3AcTJk/.../Parques_y_Jardines_Inventario_de_Arboles`), a different dataset from the singular-trees catalogue, covering ordinary managed park and street trees city-wide with species and trunk circumference.
- **El Gran Capitán (sev_005)**: exactly one Eucalyptus camaldulensis recorded in the Jardines de Murillo/Santa Cruz area, 571cm circumference, close to this entry's own sourced "close to five metres around." Moved about 100m to the exact match. The inventory's own height field (26m) is far short of the two press sources' 50-60m; recorded as a data-quality note rather than used to override directly-sourced facts.
- **The Figs of Plaza del Cristo de Burgos (sev_003)**: exactly four Ficus macrophylla clustered together in the Alfalfa neighbourhood right around the plaza, matching this entry's own count of four relocated figs. Moved about 230m to the cluster's centroid. Flagged honestly: all four measure larger (780-1020cm) than the register's own 720cm figure for "the biggest," plausible growth since an unspecified survey date, but real enough to note rather than bury.
- **Two checked and correctly left alone**: the Centennial Hackberries of María Luisa (sev_006) sit among 100 Celtis australis across the park with no age or heritage flag in this general inventory to isolate the "centennial" ones from ordinary plantings, a genuinely scattered ensemble like the Bois de la Cambre oaks; the Camphor Tree of the Glorieta de Doña Sol (sev_010) has zero Cinnamomum in this inventory at all, consistent with its existing single-source caveat that it may not be a catalogued specimen.
- **The Fig of the Glorieta de Goya (sev_009) tried and NOT resolved, on purpose**: four Ficus macrophylla candidates exist nearby but the entry cites no circumference to disambiguate them, unlike the Cristo de Burgos case where the count itself (four) was the match. Left approximate rather than guess which one.
- **Two confirmed out of this inventory's scope, not re-tried**: the Ginkgo Grove of the Real Alcázar (sev_001, zero Ginkgo found in a box covering the whole Alcázar) and, by the same jurisdictional logic, the Ombú of La Cartuja (sev_002) and the Orange Trees of the Patio de los Naranjos (sev_008), all on royal, museum or cathedral grounds a city parks department would not inventory.
- Seville now at 7 of 10 approximate pins (from 9). Photos unchanged at 5/10.
- Site rebuilt, all contracts validated, pushed.

## 2026-07-27 — Antwerp's remaining 3 approximate pins re-checked against the city's own tree layer, confirmed structurally unresolvable rather than just unsearched

- Queried Stad Antwerpen's Groeninventaris ArcGIS layer (`geodata.antwerpen.be/arcgissql/rest/services/P_Groeninventaris/Groeninventaris_extern/MapServer/5`) directly by WGS84 envelope (the service reprojects on its own, no manual EPSG:31370 math needed here, unlike Brussels' WFS) for the three gaps a 2026-07-26 pass left open.
- **The Summer Linden of Rivierenhof (ant_002)**: even a tight 300m box centred on Sterckshof castle, exactly where the story places the tree, returns 21 separate Tilia platyphyllos (73-202cm circumference) with nothing to single one out; this is a genuinely dense planting, not a search gap.
- **The Black Mulberry of Hertoghepark (ant_004)**: confirmed again, no Morus of any kind in the city's managed layer near the park, consistent with the 2026-07-26 finding that it sits outside this registry's scope (likely a garden specimen the city doesn't inventory).
- **The Cut-Leaved Horse Chestnut of Middelheim (ant_005)**: the layer's species field carries no cultivar distinction (`GENUS` is null on every record), so even with over 1,000 trees in the query box, "Aesculus hippocastanum 'Laciniata'" specifically cannot be isolated from the 77 ordinary horse chestnuts nearby. A structural limit of this data source, not a missed search.
- No data changed. Recording the specific reason for each so a future pass doesn't re-run the same three queries expecting a different result.

## 2026-07-26 — Edinburgh: a new council tree dataset tried, genuinely doesn't cover this city's entries; one near-miss caught and correctly rejected

- Found a new source not tried before: City of Edinburgh Council's own "All Edinburgh Trees" layer (a public ArcGIS FeatureServer, merged with University of Edinburgh campus data), with per-tree Latin name, common name, diameter at breast height and age group, the same class of source that worked today for Antwerp and Brussels.
- It doesn't help here. Edinburgh's ten entries are almost all historic estates and institutions (Craigmillar Castle under Historic Environment Scotland, Lauriston Castle, the Royal Botanic Garden, which is a separate Scottish Government body, not the University of Edinburgh) rather than street or park trees a city council manages, and queries at all of them came back with zero matching records, a real result, not a tooling failure (the same query mechanics returned hundreds of trees in a random city-centre test box).
- **One real near-miss caught and correctly rejected**: at Corstorphine Old Parish Church, the dataset has 5 sycamores, one clearly the local champion (90+cm trunk diameter, "Mature"). This project's own entry, though, is explicit that the current Corstorphine Sycamore is a young clone planted after the 400-year-old original died in 2005, under 25 years old. A 90cm-trunk mature tree cannot be a sub-25-year sapling, so this is a different, older sycamore in the same churchyard, not a match. Left approximate rather than misapply a real dataset match to the wrong specimen.
- No pin or photo changed. Edinburgh's photo gaps remain genuinely exhausted per the 2026-07-23/24/26 passes; this closes out a pin-resolution attempt too. Worth trying RBGE's own Living Collection database (data.rbge.org.uk, CC BY-NC-SA so lead-list only) directly for the Sweet Chestnut and Cedar of Lebanon in a future pass, not attempted this session.

- Followed up the dead-end sweep above (same session) by trying Italy's national monumental tree registry (MASAF) on Bologna, untouched today and 10/10 approximate. - **Resolved to confirmed**: the Himalayan Cedar of the Rizzoli (bol_005, AMI code 03/A944/BO/08, "Istituto Ortopedico Rizzoli - Via Pupilli 1", moved about 130m, a real correction not just tightening), the Oriental Plane of Piazza Malpighi (bol_004, AMI code 14/A944/BO/08, "Palazzo Rusconi - Piazza Malpighi 16", moved 15m, essentially confirming the existing estimate), and the Ginkgo of Piazza Cavour (bol_006, AMI code 12/A944/BO/08, "Piazza Cavour", moved 22m). All three matched on species, locality name and comune, and all three coordinates were identical (to the arcsecond) across two separate fetches.
- **One dropped for exactly the reliability reason flagged above**: AMI code 04/A944/BO/08, which should be the Plane Tree of Piazza Minghetti (bol_003) by locality name, came back three different ways across three fetches (Piazza Minghetti/unspecified species; no locality at all, coordinates 500m away; Piazza Minghetti but species "Ginco"/Ginkgo, which would be a second, different tree at the same square). ## 2026-07-26 — Madrid: 6 of 10 approximate pins resolved via OpenStreetMap's community mapping of the Comunidad de Madrid singular-tree catalogue

- Moved on to "the rest of the lead group and marquee Europe" per rung 5, after Amsterdam/Istanbul (Lisbon, Rome and Barcelona already had passes today). Madrid was untouched today: 4/10 photos, 10/10 approximate.
- Found a strong new source class: nine of Madrid's ten trees are on the Comunidad de Madrid's official "árboles singulares" register, and OpenStreetMap volunteers have been mapping that exact register as individually named tree nodes (`ES:Comunidad_de_Madrid/Árboles_singulares` on the OSM wiki, cross-checked against direct Overpass queries). Each node name is a near-exact match to this project's own tree names, which makes misidentification unlikely.
- **Resolved to confirmed**: the Ahuehuete of the Parterre (mad_001, moved ~530m to node 4565394685, independently corroborated by a separate ficha giving near-identical coordinates), the Three-Trunked Yew of the Velazquez Palace (mad_003, moved ~90m to node 3497610360, "Tejo del Palacio de Velázquez del Retiro", an exact name match to this tree's own Spanish Wikipedia article), the Bald Cypresses of the Crystal Palace Pond (mad_004, moved ~250m to node 3919471940, which at least pins the one specimen OSM has mapped under that exact name, though which physical trunk in the pond group that corresponds to is still not independently surveyed), the Cypress of the Royal Botanic Garden (mad_005, moved from the garden's street entrance to its own node 3170278091 inside the garden), the Caucasian Elm of the Botanic Garden (mad_006, moved to node 3170278093, species tag Zelkova carpinifolia matching this entry), and the Grandfather Cedar of Parque del Oeste (mad_009, moved ~300m to node 10261810384, the only tree in the park tagged "Cedro del Atlas").
- **Four gaps stayed open**: the Himalayan Cedar of the Crystal Palace (mad_002) and the Aleppo Pine of Campo del Moro (mad_008) are on the singular-tree list per its own fichas but have no matching OSM node found; the Holm Oak of the Trillo Roundabout (mad_007) already had a reasonably tight DMS-derived pin and no better source turned up; the Stone Pine of El Capricho (mad_010) is not on the regional singular-tree catalogue at all and no equivalent source was found.

## 2026-07-26 — Amsterdam: the Amstelveld pin was actually wrong, not just approximate, and is now fixed; both approximate pins cleared
